```json
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Curupira adalah roh penjaga hutan dalam mitos Tupí-Guaraní yang menghukum penebang hutan berlebihan dan dikenal dengan kakinya yang menghadap ke belakang.",
    "en": "Curupira is a forest guardian spirit of Tupí-Guaraní myth who punishes people for overcutting and is known for its backward-facing feet.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "curupira-c01",
      "curupira-c02",
      "curupira-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Curupira adalah roh hutan dalam mitos daerah berbahasa Tupí-Guaraní di Brasil, Paraguay, dan kawasan Guyana. Ia dianggap penjaga hutan dan satwa liar, dan menghukum orang yang menebang pohon secara berlebihan.",
      "en": "The Curupira is a forest spirit in the myths of Tupí-Guaraní-speaking areas of Brazil, Paraguay and the Guyanas. It is regarded as a protector of forests and wildlife and punishes people who cut too many trees.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "curupira-c01",
        "curupira-c02",
        "curupira-c10"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kakinya menghadap ke belakang, sehingga jejaknya membawa pelacak ke arah yang salah. Sebagian orang mengatakan giginya biru atau hijau, dan sumber lain menggambarkan rambutnya merah menyala.",
      "en": "Its feet point backwards, so its footprints lead trackers the wrong way. Some say its teeth are blue or green, and other accounts give it flame-red hair.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "curupira-c03",
        "curupira-c07",
        "curupira-c11"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Penyebutan tertua yang tercatat berasal dari Joseph of Anchieta pada 30 Mei 1560 di São Vicente, São Paulo. Para Yesuit awal menggambarkannya sebagai makhluk menakutkan yang ditenangkan dengan sesaji. Di bawah pengaruh Eropa, sosoknya kemudian bergeser menjadi roh penipu yang lebih jenaka.",
      "en": "The earliest recorded mention comes from Joseph of Anchieta on 30 May 1560 in São Vicente, São Paulo. Early Jesuits described it as a fearsome being appeased with offerings. Under European influence the figure later shifted toward a more mischievous trickster.",
      "claim_ids": [

        "curupira-c05",
        "curupira-c06"
      ]

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "curupira-c01",
      "source_id": "curupira-s1",
      "quote": "is a forest spirit in the myth of the Tupí-Guaraní speaking areas in the Brazil, Paraguay and Guyanas.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Curupira adalah roh hutan dalam mitos daerah berbahasa Tupí-Guaraní di Brasil, Paraguay, dan kawasan Guyana.",
        "en": "Curupira is a forest spirit in the myths of Tupí-Guaraní-speaking areas of Brazil, Paraguay and the Guyanas."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "curupira-c02",
      "source_id": "curupira-s1",
      "quote": "It is a guardian of the forests that punishes humans for overcutting.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia penjaga hutan yang menghukum manusia yang menebang pohon berlebihan.",
        "en": "It is a guardian of the forests that punishes people for overcutting."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "curupira-c03",
      "source_id": "curupira-s1",
      "quote": "Notably, the Curupira has his feet turned backwards, to mislead trackers with footprints proceeding in the opposite direction",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kaki Curupira menghadap ke belakang sehingga jejaknya menyesatkan pelacak ke arah yang berlawanan.",
        "en": "The Curupira's feet point backwards, so its footprints mislead trackers in the opposite direction."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "curupira-c04",
      "source_id": "curupira-s1",
      "quote": "The first attested mention of Curupira was in May 30th, 1560 in São Vicente, São Paulo, Brazil by Joseph of Anchieta.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penyebutan tertua Curupira yang tercatat dibuat Joseph of Anchieta pada 30 Mei 1560 di São Vicente, São Paulo.",
        "en": "The earliest recorded mention of the Curupira was by Joseph of Anchieta on 30 May 1560 in São Vicente, São Paulo."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "curupira-c05",
      "source_id": "curupira-s1",
      "quote": "The Curupira according to early Jesuits in São Paulo was a feared being known to leave gruesomely scarred bodies, to be appeased by offerings.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut para Yesuit awal di São Paulo, Curupira makhluk yang ditakuti, meninggalkan mayat penuh luka mengerikan, dan ditenangkan dengan sesaji.",
        "en": "According to early Jesuits in São Paulo, the Curupira was a feared being that left gruesomely scarred bodies and was appeased with offerings."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "curupira-c06",
      "source_id": "curupira-s1",
      "quote": "But it underwent a mutation via European influence, and was recast into more of a mischievous trickster type spirit",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di bawah pengaruh Eropa, sosoknya berubah menjadi roh penipu yang lebih jenaka.",
        "en": "Under European influence the figure changed into a more mischievous trickster spirit."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "curupira-c07",
      "source_id": "curupira-s1",
      "quote": "Some say it has blue or green teeth.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian orang mengatakan giginya berwarna biru atau hijau.",
        "en": "Some say it has blue or green teeth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "curupira-c08",
      "source_id": "curupira-s1",
      "quote": "The name Curupira means \"covered in wounds or blisters\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Curupira berarti \"dipenuhi luka atau lepuh\".",
        "en": "The name Curupira means \"covered in wounds or blisters\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "curupira-c09",
      "source_id": "curupira-s1",
      "quote": "Some commentators have argued the Curupira and Caipora to be the same, others say they are different.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian penulis menganggap Curupira dan Caipora sama, sebagian lain menganggapnya berbeda.",
        "en": "Some commentators consider the Curupira and the Caipora the same being; others say they differ."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "curupira-c10",
      "source_id": "curupira-s2",
      "quote": "Er gilt als Beschützer der Wälder und der wilden Tiere.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia dianggap pelindung hutan dan satwa liar.",
        "en": "It is regarded as the protector of forests and wild animals."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "curupira-c11",
      "source_id": "curupira-s2",
      "quote": "Weitere Merkmale sind flammendrote oder feurige Haare beschrieben",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ciri lain yang digambarkan adalah rambut merah menyala atau berapi-api.",
        "en": "Other described features are flame-red or fiery hair."
      }
```

```json
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Ular Berbulu adalah dewa atau sosok supernatural penting dalam banyak agama Mesoamerika, dikenal sebagai Quetzalcōātl oleh orang Aztek dan Kukulkan oleh Maya Yukatek.",
    "en": "The Feathered Serpent is a prominent deity or supernatural entity of many Mesoamerican religions, known as Quetzalcōātl to the Aztecs and Kukulkan to the Yucatec Maya.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "feathered-serpent-c01",
      "feathered-serpent-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ular Berbulu adalah sosok ilahi yang dipuja dalam banyak agama Mesoamerika. Orang Aztek menyebutnya Quetzalcōātl, Maya Yukatek menyebutnya Kukulkan, dan Maya Kʼicheʼ mengenalnya sebagai Qʼuqʼumatz dan Tohil. Bulunya ditafsirkan sebagai lambang sifat ilahi dan kemampuan terbang, sedangkan tubuh ularnya sebagai lambang sifat manusiawi yang melata di bumi.",
      "en": "The Feathered Serpent is a divine figure revered in many Mesoamerican religions. The Aztecs called it Quetzalcōātl, the Yucatec Maya Kukulkan, and the Kʼicheʼ Maya knew it as Qʼuqʼumatz and Tohil. Its feathers are read as a sign of divine nature and flight, its serpent body as a sign of a human nature that creeps on the earth.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "feathered-serpent-c01",
        "feathered-serpent-c02",
        "feathered-serpent-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Gambar ular berbulu sudah muncul dalam kebudayaan Olmek (sekitar 1400–400 SM), biasanya berupa ular derik berjambul. Di Teotihuacan (200 SM – 700 M) sosok ini menonjol di Kuil Ular Berbulu, dan secara umum paling sering tampil dalam arsitektur Mesoamerika.",
      "en": "Images of feathered serpents already appear in Olmec culture (c. 1400–400 BC), usually as a crested rattlesnake. At Teotihuacan (200 BC – 700 AD) the figure is prominent on the Temple of the Feathered Serpent, and overall it appears most often in Mesoamerican architecture.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "feathered-serpent-c04",
        "feathered-serpent-c05",
        "feathered-serpent-c06",
        "feathered-serpent-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut legenda, Ular Berbulu menguasai bintang pagi, menciptakan buku dan kalender, serta memberi jagung kepada manusia. Ia juga melambangkan kematian dan kelahiran kembali, dan menjadi pelindung para pendeta.",
      "en": "According to legend, the Feathered Serpent rules the morning star, invented books and the calendar, and gave maize to humanity. It also stands for death and rebirth and is the patron of priests.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "feathered-serpent-c08",
        "feathered-serpent-c09"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "statement": {
        "id": "Ular Berbulu adalah sosok supernatural atau dewa penting dalam banyak agama Mesoamerika.",
        "en": "The Feathered Serpent is a prominent supernatural entity or deity in many Mesoamerican religions."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "feathered-serpent-c02",
      "source_id": "feathered-serpent-s1",
      "quote": "It is called Quetzalcōātl among the Aztecs; Kukulkan among the Yucatec Maya; and Qʼuqʼumatz and Tohil among the Kʼicheʼ Maya.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia disebut Quetzalcōātl oleh orang Aztek, Kukulkan oleh Maya Yukatek, serta Qʼuqʼumatz dan Tohil oleh Maya Kʼicheʼ.",
        "en": "It is called Quetzalcōātl by the Aztecs, Kukulkan by the Yucatec Maya, and Qʼuqʼumatz and Tohil by the Kʼicheʼ Maya."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "feathered-serpent-c03",
      "source_id": "feathered-serpent-s1",
      "quote": "being feathered represents its divine nature or ability to fly to reach the skies, while being a serpent represents its human nature or ability to creep on the ground",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bulu melambangkan sifat ilahinya atau kemampuan terbang ke langit, sedangkan wujud ular melambangkan sifat manusiawi atau kemampuan melata di tanah.",
        "en": "Its feathers represent its divine nature or ability to fly to the skies, while its serpent form represents its human nature or ability to creep on the ground."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "feathered-serpent-c04",
      "source_id": "feathered-serpent-s1",
      "quote": "Representations of feathered serpents appear in the Olmec culture (c. 1400–400 BC).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penggambaran ular berbulu sudah muncul dalam kebudayaan Olmek (sekitar 1400–400 SM).",
        "en": "Representations of feathered serpents appear in Olmec culture (c. 1400–400 BC)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "feathered-serpent-c05",
      "source_id": "feathered-serpent-s1",
      "quote": "show the Feathered Serpent as a crested rattlesnake, sometimes with feathers covering the body and legs, and often close to humans.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Seni Olmek menggambarkannya sebagai ular derik berjambul, kadang dengan bulu menutupi tubuh dan kaki, dan sering berada dekat manusia.",
        "en": "Olmec art shows it as a crested rattlesnake, sometimes with feathers covering the body and legs, often close to humans."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "feathered-serpent-c06",
      "source_id": "feathered-serpent-s1",
      "quote": "The pantheon of the people of Teotihuacan (200 BC – 700 AD) also featured a feathered serpent, shown most prominently on the Temple of the Feathered Serpent",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jajaran dewa Teotihuacan (200 SM – 700 M) juga memuat ular berbulu, yang paling menonjol di Kuil Ular Berbulu.",
        "en": "The pantheon of Teotihuacan (200 BC – 700 AD) also included a feathered serpent, most prominently on the Temple of the Feathered Serpent."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "feathered-serpent-c07",
      "source_id": "feathered-serpent-s1",
      "quote": "it is frequently and most commonly reflected in the architecture of Mesoamerican culture",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ular Berbulu paling sering tampil dalam arsitektur Mesoamerika.",
        "en": "The Feathered Serpent appears most often in Mesoamerican architecture."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "feathered-serpent-c08",
      "source_id": "feathered-serpent-s2",
      "quote": "按照传说，羽蛇主宰着晨星、发明书籍、曆法，而且给人类带来玉米。",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut legenda, Ular Berbulu menguasai bintang pagi, menciptakan buku dan kalender, serta membawa jagung bagi manusia.",
        "en": "According to legend, the Feathered Serpent rules the morning star, invented books and the calendar, and brought maize to humanity."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "feathered-serpent-c09",
      "source_id": "feathered-serpent-s2",
      "quote": "曆法，而且给人类带来玉米。羽蛇神还代表着死亡和重生，是祭司的守护神。",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ular Berbulu juga melambangkan kematian dan kelahiran kembali, serta menjadi pelindung para pendeta.",
        "en": "The Feathered Serpent also stands for death and rebirth and is the patron of priests."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",

    }
  ]
}
```

```json
      "en": "Nahuelito is a lake monster said to inhabit Nahuel Huapi Lake in Patagonia, Argentina. It is described as a giant serpent, a huge hump or a plesiosaur. The legend is thought to be rooted in indigenous stories from before the colonization of the Americas.",
```

```json
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Ahuizotl adalah makhluk air dalam mitologi Aztek, sebesar anjing kecil dengan tangan di ujung ekornya, yang konon menyeret orang dari tepi air.",
    "en": "The ahuizotl is a water creature of Aztec mythology, the size of a small dog with a hand at the end of its tail, said to drag people from the water's edge.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "ahuizotl-mythology-c01",
      "ahuizotl-mythology-c04",
      "ahuizotl-mythology-c07",
      "ahuizotl-mythology-c08"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ahuizotl adalah makhluk legendaris dalam mitologi Aztek. Ia digambarkan sebesar anjing kecil dengan bulu kedap air, dan namanya dalam bahasa Nahuatl kurang lebih berarti \"anjing air\". Makhluk ini tercatat dalam Buku 11 Codex Florentine.",
      "en": "The ahuizotl is a legendary creature of Aztec mythology. It is described as the size of a small dog with waterproof fur, and its Nahuatl name roughly means \"water dog\". It is recorded in Book 11 of the Florentine Codex.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ahuizotl-mythology-c01",
        "ahuizotl-mythology-c04",
        "ahuizotl-mythology-c05",
        "ahuizotl-mythology-c09"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut kebanyakan kisah, ahuizotl menyambar orang dari tepi air dengan tangan di ujung ekornya, lalu memakan mata, kuku, dan gigi mereka. Dalam beberapa versi, dewa Tlaloc dan Chalchiuhtlicue mengutusnya untuk mengambil jiwa manusia yang mereka sukai.",
      "en": "By most accounts the ahuizotl seized people from the water's edge with the hand on its tail and ate their eyes, nails and teeth. In some versions the gods Tlaloc and Chalchiuhtlicue sent it to collect the souls of mortals they favoured.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ahuizotl-mythology-c06",
        "ahuizotl-mythology-c07",
        "ahuizotl-mythology-c08"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Penguasa Aztek Auítzotl mengambil namanya dari makhluk ini dan menjadikannya lambang serta sosok pelindung, sehingga ahuizotl disebut sahabat dewa-dewa hujan. Hernán Cortés bahkan menulis kepada Raja Kastilia bahwa ahuizotl telah menewaskan seorang prajuritnya.",
      "en": "The Aztec ruler Auítzotl took his name from the creature and made it his emblem and protective figure, and the ahuizotl was called a friend of the rain gods. Hernán Cortés even wrote to the King of Castile that an ahuizotl had killed one of his soldiers.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ahuizotl-mythology-c02",
        "ahuizotl-mythology-c03",
        "ahuizotl-mythology-c10"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "ahuizotl-mythology-c01",
      "source_id": "ahuizotl-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "is a legendary creature in Aztec mythology. It is said to lure people to their deaths.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ahuizotl adalah makhluk legendaris dalam mitologi Aztek yang konon memikat orang menuju kematian.",
        "en": "The ahuizotl is a legendary creature of Aztec mythology said to lure people to their deaths."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ahuizotl-mythology-c02",
      "source_id": "ahuizotl-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "The creature was taken as an emblem by the ruler of the same name, and was said to be a \"friend of the rain gods\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penguasa yang bernama sama menjadikannya lambang, dan makhluk ini disebut \"sahabat dewa-dewa hujan\".",
        "en": "The ruler of the same name took it as his emblem, and it was called a \"friend of the rain gods\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ahuizotl-mythology-c03",
      "source_id": "ahuizotl-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "The conquistador Hernán Cortés once reported to the King of Castile that one of his men had been killed by an ahuizotl.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hernán Cortés pernah melapor kepada Raja Kastilia bahwa salah satu anak buahnya dibunuh ahuizotl.",
        "en": "Hernán Cortés once reported to the King of Castile that one of his men had been killed by an ahuizotl."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ahuizotl-mythology-c04",
      "source_id": "ahuizotl-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "The creature is described as being the size of a small dog, with waterproof fur.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Makhluk ini digambarkan sebesar anjing kecil dengan bulu kedap air.",
        "en": "It is described as the size of a small dog, with waterproof fur."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ahuizotl-mythology-c05",
      "source_id": "ahuizotl-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "The ahuizotl was included within Book 11 of the Florentine Codex",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ahuizotl tercatat dalam Buku 11 Codex Florentine.",
        "en": "The ahuizotl is recorded in Book 11 of the Florentine Codex."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ahuizotl-mythology-c06",
      "source_id": "ahuizotl-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "In other versions, it was sent by the gods Tlaloc and Chalchiuhtlicue to collect the souls of mortals they liked.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam beberapa versi, dewa Tlaloc dan Chalchiuhtlicue mengutusnya untuk mengambil jiwa manusia yang mereka sukai.",
        "en": "In some versions the gods Tlaloc and Chalchiuhtlicue sent it to collect the souls of mortals they favoured."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ahuizotl-mythology-c07",
      "source_id": "ahuizotl-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "By most accounts, the ahuizotl would snatch people from the water's edge, so that it could feast on their eyes, nails, and teeth.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut kebanyakan kisah, ahuizotl menyambar orang dari tepi air untuk memakan mata, kuku, dan gigi mereka.",
        "en": "By most accounts the ahuizotl snatched people from the water's edge to feast on their eyes, nails and teeth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ahuizotl-mythology-c08",
      "source_id": "ahuizotl-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "The ahuizotl was said to use the hand on the end of its tail to snatch its prey.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ahuizotl konon memakai tangan di ujung ekornya untuk menyambar mangsa.",
        "en": "The ahuizotl was said to snatch its prey with the hand at the end of its tail."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ahuizotl-mythology-c09",
      "source_id": "ahuizotl-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "Der Name kommt aus dem Nahuatl und bedeutet so viel wie „Wasserhund“.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya berasal dari bahasa Nahuatl dan kurang lebih berarti \"anjing air\".",
        "en": "The name comes from Nahuatl and roughly means \"water dog\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ahuizotl-mythology-c10",
      "source_id": "ahuizotl-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "Der Aztekenherrscher Auítzotl hatte seinen Namen vom Fabelwesen Ahuitzotl angenommen, das er als seine Schutzfigur sah.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penguasa Aztek Auítzotl mengambil namanya dari makhluk ini dan memandangnya sebagai sosok pelindungnya.",
        "en": "The Aztec ruler Auítzotl took his name from the creature and saw it as his protective figure."
      }
    }
  ],
```

```json
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Caipora adalah roh hutan dalam cerita rakyat Brasil yang menjaga satwa liar dari pemburu dan sering digambarkan menunggang peccary.",
    "en": "Caipora is a forest spirit of Brazilian folklore who guards wildlife from hunters and is often shown riding a peccary.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Caipora adalah roh hutan atau makhluk mirip manusia dalam cerita rakyat Brasil yang menjaga satwa liar dan hewan buruan. Namanya berasal dari bahasa Tupi dan berarti \"penghuni hutan\". Luís da Câmara Cascudo berpendapat Caapora adalah bentuk maskulin dan Caipora bentuk femininnya.",
      "en": "Caipora is a forest spirit or humanoid of Brazilian folklore that guards wildlife and game. Its name comes from Tupi and means \"inhabitant of the forest\". Luís da Câmara Cascudo argued that Caapora is the masculine form and Caipora the feminine.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "caipora-c01",
        "caipora-c02",
        "caipora-c08"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Wujudnya beragam: anak pribumi kecil berkulit gelap, kerdil, atau makhluk berbulu bertubuh besar. Ia sering menunggang peccary dan membawa tongkat atau cambuk dari tumbuhan tertentu. Caipora menerima suap tembakau dan minuman keras; menurut satu sumber, ia meminta tembakau untuk pipanya dan berbuat iseng jahat bila tidak diberi.",
      "en": "Its form varies: a small dark-skinned Native child, a dwarf, or a large hairy humanoid or beast. It often rides a peccary and carries a stick or whip made of certain plants. Caipora accepts bribes of tobacco and alcohol; one source says she asks for tobacco for her pipe and plays malicious pranks if refused.",

        "caipora-c10"

    {
      "id": "Caipora kadang dikacaukan dengan Curupira, roh pelindung hutan lainnya. Dalam bahasa sehari-hari, kata Caipora juga dipakai secara kiasan untuk orang yang dianggap membawa pengaruh buruk.",
```

```json
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Demon Cat adalah hantu kucing dalam cerita rakyat Amerika yang konon menghantui Gedung Kapitol dan Gedung Putih di Washington, D.C.",
    "en": "The Demon Cat is a ghost cat of American folklore said to haunt the Capitol and the White House in Washington, D.C.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "demon-cat-c01",
      "demon-cat-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Demon Cat adalah hantu kucing yang konon menghantui gedung-gedung pemerintahan di Washington, D.C., terutama Gedung Putih dan Gedung Kapitol. Kisahnya bermula dari kucing-kucing yang dibawa ke terowongan bawah tanah Kapitol untuk memburu tikus; menurut legenda, salah satunya tidak pernah pergi meski sudah mati.",
      "en": "The Demon Cat is a ghost cat said to haunt government buildings in Washington, D.C., especially the White House and the Capitol. The story starts with cats brought into the Capitol's basement tunnels to hunt rats; legend says one of them never left, even after death.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "demon-cat-c01",
        "demon-cat-c02",
        "demon-cat-c03",
        "demon-cat-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kucing ini digambarkan sebagai kucing hitam atau belang seukuran kucing rumah, tetapi saksi melaporkan ia membesar hingga seukuran harimau raksasa atau gajah. Ia dipercaya muncul menjelang pemilihan presiden dan tragedi, termasuk sebelum pembunuhan John Kennedy dan Abraham Lincoln serta krisis Wall Street 1929. Pada tahun 1890-an ia konon lenyap saat ditembaki penjaga Capitol Hill.",
      "en": "The cat is described as a black or tabby house-sized cat, but witnesses say it swells to the size of a giant tiger or an elephant. It is believed to appear before presidential elections and tragedies, including the assassinations of John Kennedy and Abraham Lincoln and the 1929 Wall Street crash. In the 1890s it reportedly vanished when Capitol Hill guards shot at it.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "demon-cat-c05",
        "demon-cat-c06",
        "demon-cat-c07",
        "demon-cat-c08",
        "demon-cat-c11"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Steve Livengood dari U.S. Capitol Historical Society menduga legenda ini berawal dari penjaga yang mabuk, yang dijilat kucing Kapitol lalu mengira kucing itu raksasa.",
      "en": "Steve Livengood of the U.S. Capitol Historical Society suggests the legend began with a drunken guard who was licked by a Capitol cat and took it for a giant.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "demon-cat-c09",
        "demon-cat-c10"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "id": "demon-cat-c03",
      "source_id": "demon-cat-s1",
      "quote": "cats were brought into the basement tunnels of the United States Capitol Building to catch rats and mice",
      "locator": null,

      "statement": {

    },
    {

      "source_id": "demon-cat-s1",
      "quote": "Legend states that the Demon Cat is one of these cats that never left, even after its death.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut legenda, Demon Cat adalah salah satu kucing itu yang tidak pernah pergi, bahkan setelah mati.",

      }
    },

      "id": "demon-cat-c05",
      "source_id": "demon-cat-s1",
      "quote": "According to legend, the cat is seen before presidential elections and tragedies in Washington, D.C.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut legenda, kucing ini terlihat menjelang pemilihan presiden dan tragedi di Washington, D.C.",

      "id": "demon-cat-c06",
      "source_id": "demon-cat-s1",
      "quote": "It is described as either a black cat or a tabby cat, and the size of an average house cat.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia digambarkan sebagai kucing hitam atau kucing belang seukuran kucing rumah biasa.",

    {
      "id": "demon-cat-c07",
      "source_id": "demon-cat-s1",
      "quote": "witnesses report that the cat swells to \"the size of a giant tiger\" or an elephant",

      "statement": {

      }
    },
    {
      "id": "demon-cat-c08",
      "source_id": "demon-cat-s1",
      "quote": "In the 1890s, the cat is said to have inexplicably vanished when some Capitol Hill guards fired their guns at it",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada tahun 1890-an, kucing itu konon lenyap secara misterius saat beberapa penjaga Capitol Hill menembakinya.",

    },
    {
      "id": "demon-cat-c09",
      "source_id": "demon-cat-s1",
      "quote": "According to Steve Livengood, the chief tour guide of the U.S. Capitol Historical Society, the Capitol Police force was notorious for hiring unqualified relatives and friends of Congressmen as favors",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Steve Livengood, pemandu utama U.S. Capitol Historical Society, kepolisian Kapitol dulu terkenal merekrut kerabat dan kawan anggota Kongres yang tidak cakap.",

      "source_id": "demon-cat-s1",
      "quote": "Livengood believes the legend began when a security guard who was lying down in a drunken stupor was licked by one of the Capitol building's cats and mistakenly assumed it to be a giant cat.",
```

```json
    "id": "Lizard Man of Scape Ore Swamp adalah kriptid mirip kadal yang konon menghuni rawa di Lee County, South Carolina, dan terkenal sejak akhir 1980-an.",
    "en": "The Lizard Man of Scape Ore Swamp is a lizard-like cryptid said to inhabit the swamps of Lee County, South Carolina, famous since the late 1980s.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Lee County, South Carolina, Lizard Man adalah makhluk yang konon menghuni rawa Scape Ore. Ia digambarkan berjalan dengan dua kaki, kekar, berjari tiga, bermata merah, dan berkulit bersisik seperti kadal atau ular.",
      "en": "In the folklore of Lee County, South Carolina, the Lizard Man is an entity said to live in the Scape Ore swamp. It is described as bipedal and burly, with three fingers, red eyes and lizard- or snake-like scales.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "lizard-man-of-scape-ore-swamp-c01",
        "lizard-man-of-scape-ore-swamp-c05",

    {
      "id": "Kisahnya mencuat pada 1988. Pada 14 Juli tahun itu sheriff Lee County menyelidiki laporan mobil yang rusak semalaman, lalu Christopher Davis yang berusia 17 tahun melapor bahwa mobilnya dirusak makhluk hijau setinggi sekitar 7 kaki. Liputan media memicu laporan lain, dan stasiun radio WCOS menawarkan hadiah 1 juta dolar bagi yang menangkapnya hidup-hidup.",
      "en": "The story took off in 1988. On 14 July that year the Lee County sheriff investigated a car damaged overnight, and 17-year-old Christopher Davis then reported that a green creature about 7 feet tall had damaged his car.         "id": "Makhluk itu disebut berjari tiga, bermata merah, berkulit seperti kadal, dan bersisik seperti ular.",
        "en": "The creature was said to have three fingers, red eyes, lizard-like skin and snakelike scales."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lizard-man-of-scape-ore-swamp-c06",
      "source_id": "lizard-man-of-scape-ore-swamp-s1",
      "quote": "Local radio station WCOS offered a $1 million reward to anybody who could capture the creature alive.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Stasiun radio lokal WCOS menawarkan hadiah 1 juta dolar bagi siapa pun yang menangkapnya hidup-hidup.",
        "en": "Local radio station WCOS offered a $1 million reward for capturing the creature alive."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lizard-man-of-scape-ore-swamp-c07",
      "source_id": "lizard-man-of-scape-ore-swamp-s1",
      "quote": "Professor of religious studies Joseph P. Laycock described the media frenzy and subsequent cult appreciation for this and other similar claims as following a predictable \"chain of events – a strange sighting, media attention, more sightings, followed by visits from curious tourists and monster hunters\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Profesor studi agama Joseph P. Laycock menilai heboh media seputar klaim ini mengikuti pola yang bisa ditebak: penampakan aneh, perhatian media, penampakan lain, lalu kunjungan turis dan pemburu monster.",
        "en": "Religious studies professor Joseph P. Laycock described the media frenzy around this claim as a predictable chain: a strange sighting, media attention, more sightings, then curious tourists and monster hunters."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lizard-man-of-scape-ore-swamp-c08",
      "source_id": "lizard-man-of-scape-ore-swamp-s1",
      "quote": "Researcher Ben Radford states that this is a compelling story, \"but several important aspects wither under skeptical scrutiny\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Peneliti Ben Radford menyebutnya kisah menarik, tetapi beberapa aspek pentingnya runtuh bila diperiksa secara skeptis.",
        "en": "Researcher Ben Radford calls it a compelling story, but says several important aspects wither under skeptical scrutiny."
      }
    },

      "quote": "bipedal, dan kekar, dengan kulit bersisik hijau dan mata merah menyala.",

        "en": "The Lizard Man is described as bipedal and burly, with green scaly skin and glowing red eyes."
      }
```

```json
      "en": "The Loveland frog is a legendary humanoid of Loveland, Ohio. The legend began with a 1955 sighting: a driver leaving Branch Hill saw three leathery, frog-faced figures standing by the road, and in other versions one of them held a wand that shot sparks.",

      "source_id": "loveland-frog-s1",
      "quote": "The legend originated with an alleged sighting in 1955 and gained renewed attention in 1972, when a Loveland police officer reported encountering an animal resembling the frogman.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda ini bermula dari penampakan tahun 1955 dan kembali ramai pada 1972, ketika seorang polisi Loveland melapor bertemu hewan mirip manusia katak.",

      "id": "loveland-frog-c03",
      "source_id": "loveland-frog-s1",
      "quote": "the driver was heading out of the Branch Hill neighborhood when he spotted three figures standing on their hind legs along the side of the road",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam satu kisah, seorang pengemudi yang keluar dari lingkungan Branch Hill melihat tiga sosok berdiri dengan kaki belakang di tepi jalan.",

    {
      "id": "loveland-frog-c04",
      "source_id": "loveland-frog-s1",
      "quote": "with leathery skin and froglike faces. In other versions of the story, they were spotted either on top of or beneath a poorly lit bridge, with one holding a wand overhead that fired a spray of sparks.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sosok-sosok itu berkulit seperti kulit samak dan berwajah katak; versi lain menempatkan mereka di atas atau di bawah jembatan remang-remang, dengan satu sosok mengangkat tongkat yang memercikkan bunga api.",
        "en": "The figures had leathery skin and frog-like faces; other versions put them on or under a dim bridge, with one holding up a wand that shot sparks."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "loveland-frog-c05",
      "source_id": "loveland-frog-s1",
      "quote": "Loveland police officer Ray Shockey was driving on Riverside Drive near the Totes boot factory and the Little Miami River when an unidentified animal scurried across the road in front of his vehicle.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Polisi Loveland Ray Shockey sedang berkendara di Riverside Drive dekat Sungai Little Miami ketika seekor hewan tak dikenal melintas di depan mobilnya.",
        "en": "Loveland police officer Ray Shockey was driving on Riverside Drive near the Little Miami River when an unidentified animal ran across the road in front of him."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "loveland-frog-c06",
      "source_id": "loveland-frog-s1",
      "quote": "He reported that the animal \"crouched like a frog\" before it momentarily stood erect to climb over the guardrail and back down towards the river.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia melaporkan hewan itu berjongkok seperti katak lalu sempat berdiri tegak untuk memanjat pagar pembatas dan turun ke sungai.",
        "en": "He reported that the animal crouched like a frog, then briefly stood upright to climb the guardrail and go back down to the river."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "loveland-frog-c07",
      "source_id": "loveland-frog-s1",
      "quote": "Two weeks after the incident, a second Loveland police officer, Mark Matthews, reported seeing an unidentified animal crouched along the road in the same vicinity as the first officer's sighting.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dua minggu kemudian, polisi Loveland kedua, Mark Matthews, melihat hewan tak dikenal berjongkok di tepi jalan di sekitar lokasi yang sama.",
        "en": "Two weeks later a second Loveland officer, Mark Matthews, saw an unidentified animal crouching by the road in the same area."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "loveland-frog-c08",
      "source_id": "loveland-frog-s1",
      "quote": "According to Matthews, Shockey was shown the dead iguana and confirmed it was the animal he had seen two weeks prior.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Matthews, Shockey ditunjukkan iguana mati itu dan membenarkan bahwa itulah hewan yang dilihatnya dua minggu sebelumnya.",
        "en": "According to Matthews, Shockey was shown the dead iguana and confirmed it was the animal he had seen two weeks earlier."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "loveland-frog-c09",
      "source_id": "loveland-frog-s1",
      "quote": "Matthews speculated that the iguana had been someone's pet that \"either got loose or was released when it grew too large\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Matthews menduga iguana itu hewan peliharaan yang lepas atau dilepas karena tumbuh terlalu besar.",
        "en": "Matthews speculated the iguana was a pet that escaped or was released when it grew too large."
```

```json
      "id": "Manipogo adalah monster danau yang konon hidup di Danau Manitoba, Kanada. Namanya meniru Ogopogo dari British Columbia.       "en": "Manipogo is a lake monster said to live in Lake Manitoba, Canada. Its name echoes British Columbia's Ogopogo.       "en": "Local First Nations people have long told of serpent-like creatures in the lake.   "claims": [
    {
      "id": "manipogo-c01",
      "source_id": "manipogo-s1",
      "quote": "In Canadian folklore, the Manipogo is a lake monster said to live in Lake Manitoba, Manitoba, Canada.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Kanada, Manipogo adalah monster danau yang konon hidup di Danau Manitoba, Kanada.",
        "en": "In Canadian folklore, Manipogo is a lake monster said to live in Lake Manitoba, Canada."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "manipogo-c02",
      "source_id": "manipogo-s1",
      "quote": "The creature was dubbed Manipogo in 1960, the name echoing British Columbia's Ogopogo.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Manipogo diberikan pada 1960 dengan meniru nama Ogopogo dari British Columbia.",
        "en": "The creature was named Manipogo in 1960, echoing British Columbia's Ogopogo."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "manipogo-c03",
      "source_id": "manipogo-s1",
      "quote": "It is the namesake of the Manipogo Provincial Park.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Taman Provinsi Manipogo dinamai menurut makhluk ini.",
        "en": "Manipogo Provincial Park is named after it."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "manipogo-c04",
      "source_id": "manipogo-s1",
      "quote": "The community of St. Laurent on the southeast shore of Lake Manitoba holds a yearly Manipogo Festival during the first week of March.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Komunitas St. Laurent di tepi tenggara Danau Manitoba mengadakan Festival Manipogo setiap minggu pertama bulan Maret.",
        "en": "The community of St. Laurent on Lake Manitoba's southeast shore holds a Manipogo Festival every first week of March."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "manipogo-c05",
      "source_id": "manipogo-s1",
      "quote": "It is described as having \"a brownish-black body and at least one hump that shows above the water.\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia digambarkan bertubuh cokelat kehitaman dengan sedikitnya satu punuk yang tampak di atas air.",
        "en": "It is described as having a brownish-black body with at least one hump showing above the water."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "manipogo-c06",
      "source_id": "manipogo-s1",
      "quote": "Its head is sometimes compared to that of a horse, camel, or sheep, while others have claimed it was flat and diamond-shaped.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kepalanya kadang disamakan dengan kepala kuda, unta, atau domba; ada pula yang menyebutnya pipih dan berbentuk belah ketupat.",
        "en": "Its head is likened to a horse's, camel's or sheep's, while others say it is flat and diamond-shaped."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "manipogo-c07",
      "source_id": "manipogo-s1",
      "quote": "It is purported to let out a shriek or cry as it surfaces",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia konon mengeluarkan jeritan saat muncul ke permukaan.",
        "en": "It reportedly shrieks or cries as it surfaces."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "manipogo-c08",
      "source_id": "manipogo-s1",
      "quote": "The local First Nations population has legends of serpent-like creatures in Lake Manitoba going back hundreds of years.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penduduk First Nations setempat punya legenda tentang makhluk mirip ular di Danau Manitoba sejak ratusan tahun lalu.",
        "en": "Local First Nations people have legends of serpent-like creatures in Lake Manitoba going back hundreds of years."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "manipogo-c09",
      "source_id": "manipogo-s1",
      "quote": "The first documented sighting by a white settler came in September 1909, when Hudson's Bay Company fur trader Valentine McKay claimed to see a huge creature in Cedar Lake near Graves Point on Lake Manitoba.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penampakan pertama yang terdokumentasi oleh pemukim kulit putih terjadi pada September 1909, ketika pedagang bulu Valentine McKay dari Hudson's Bay Company mengaku melihat makhluk besar.",
        "en": "The first documented sighting by a white settler was in September 1909, when Hudson's Bay Company fur trader Valentine McKay claimed to see a huge creature."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "manipogo-c10",
      "source_id": "manipogo-s1",
      "quote": "Other sceptics believe that the so-called monster is actually a swimming moose with its head above the surface and its humped back showing as well.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian skeptis menduga monster itu sebenarnya rusa moose yang berenang dengan kepala dan punggung bungkuknya terlihat.",
        "en": "Some sceptics think the monster is a swimming moose with its head and humped back showing."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "manipogo-c11",
      "source_id": "manipogo-s2",
      "quote": "1936年：渔民奥斯卡·弗雷德里克森（Oscar Fredrickson）在拉起一网鱼的时候，发现在网里捞起了一块动物的头盖骨。",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada 1936 nelayan Oscar Fredrickson menemukan sebuah tengkorak hewan di jaring ikannya.",
        "en": "In 1936 fisherman Oscar Fredrickson found an animal skull in his fishing net."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "manipogo-c12",
      "source_id": "manipogo-s2",
      "quote": "但不久后弗雷德里克森家发生不明原因的火灾，把整个家都烧毁了，而那块从马尼托巴湖捞起的头盖骨也下落不明。",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tak lama kemudian rumah Fredrickson terbakar tanpa sebab yang jelas, dan tengkorak itu hilang.",
        "en": "Soon after, Fredrickson's house burned down for unknown reasons and the skull was lost."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "manipogo-c13",
      "source_id": "manipogo-s2",
      "quote": "当地的土著居民也有水怪的传说。这种生物在1957年被命名为马尼波戈，以响应不列颠哥伦比亚省发现的欧戈波戈。",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penduduk asli setempat juga punya legenda monster air; menurut sumber ini, makhluk itu dinamai Manipogo pada 1957 dengan meniru Ogopogo dari British Columbia.",
        "en": "Local indigenous people also have legends of a water monster; according to this source, the creature was named Manipogo in 1957, echoing British Columbia's Ogopogo."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [
```

```json
    {
      "id": "Patasola, yang berarti \"satu kaki\", adalah monster perempuan hutan dalam cerita rakyat Kolombia. Ia mendatangi pemburu atau penebang kayu di belantara, menyamar sebagai perempuan cantik yang sering menyerupai orang terkasih, lalu memikat pria menjauh dari rekan-rekannya. Di dalam hutan ia menampakkan wujud aslinya: makhluk berkaki satu yang haus daging dan darah manusia.",
      "en": "The Patasola, meaning \"single leg\", is a female jungle monster of Colombian folklore. She appears to hunters or loggers in the wilderness disguised as a beautiful woman, often resembling a loved one, and lures a man away from his companions. Deep in the forest she reveals her true form: a one-legged creature hungry for human flesh and blood.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "patasola-c01",
        "patasola-c02",
        "patasola-c03"

    {
      "id": "Kaki tunggalnya berakhir dengan kuku belah seperti kuku sapi, dan ia dipercaya bisa berubah menjadi anjing hitam besar atau sapi. Ia menghuni pegunungan dan hutan lebat, dan biasanya dianggap pelindung alam serta satwa hutan yang tidak mengampuni perusak hutan.",
      "en": "Her single leg ends in a cloven, cow-like hoof, and she is believed to turn into a large black dog or a cow. She lives in mountains and dense forest and is usually seen as a protector of nature and forest animals who shows no mercy to those who damage them.",

      "id": "patasola-c01",
      "source_id": "patasola-s1",
      "quote": "The Patasola or \"single leg\" is one of many legends in Colombian folklore about female monster from jungle, appearing to male hunters or loggers in the middle of the wilderness if they think about women.",

    {

      "source_id": "patasola-s1",
      "quote": "According to popular belief, it inhabits mountain ranges, virgin forests, and other heavily wooded or jungle-like areas.",

      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

        "en": "Popular belief places it in mountain ranges, virgin forests and other densely wooded areas."

    },
    {
      "id": "patasola-c05",
      "source_id": "patasola-s1",
      "quote": "The patasola is usually regarded as protective of nature and the forest animals and unforgiving when humans enter their domain to alter or destroy them.",

      "statement": {

      "source_id": "patasola-s1",
      "quote": "Some believe that she was a mother who killed her own son and was then banished to the woods as punishment.",

      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
```

```json
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Tzitzimitl adalah dewi langit Aztek yang dikaitkan dengan bintang dan kesuburan, digambarkan sebagai sosok perempuan berwujud kerangka, dan ditakuti saat gerhana matahari.",
    "en": "A Tzitzimitl is an Aztec celestial goddess linked to the stars and fertility, shown as a skeletal female figure and feared during solar eclipses.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "tzitzimitl-c01",
      "tzitzimitl-c02",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Aztek, Tzitzimitl (jamak Tzitzimimeh) adalah dewi langit yang dikaitkan dengan bintang. Mereka digambarkan sebagai perempuan berwujud kerangka yang memakai rok bermotif tengkorak; dalam Codex Magliabechiano roknya berhias kerang. Uraian pascapenaklukan sering menyebut mereka iblis, tetapi sebutan itu belum tentu sesuai dengan fungsi mereka.",
      "en": "In Aztec mythology a Tzitzimitl (plural Tzitzimimeh) is a celestial goddess linked to the stars. They were shown as skeletal women wearing skirts with skull designs; in the Codex Magliabechiano the skirts are edged with shells. Post-conquest accounts often call them demons, but that label does not necessarily match their role.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tzitzimitl-c01",
        "tzitzimitl-c02",
        "tzitzimitl-c03",
        "tzitzimitl-c12"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Tzitzimimeh adalah dewi kesuburan yang dipuja bidan dan perempuan yang akan melahirkan. Pemimpin mereka adalah Itzpapalotl, penguasa surga Tamoanchan. Mereka berperan ganda sebagai pelindung kaum perempuan dan leluhur umat manusia.",
      "en": "The Tzitzimimeh were fertility goddesses worshipped by midwives and women in childbirth. Their leader was Itzpapalotl, ruler of the paradise Tamoanchan. They had a double role as protectors of women and ancestresses of humankind.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tzitzimitl-c04",
        "tzitzimitl-c05",
        "tzitzimitl-c06",
        "tzitzimitl-c10"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Mereka juga ditakuti. Bintang yang tampak saat gerhana matahari ditafsirkan sebagai Tzitzimimeh yang menyerang Matahari dan turun merasuki manusia. Bila upacara api baru di akhir siklus kalender 52 tahun gagal, mereka dipercaya akan memangsa manusia terakhir; mereka juga ditakuti pada lima hari sial Nemontemi.",
      "en": "They were also feared. Stars visible during a solar eclipse were read as Tzitzimimeh attacking the Sun and descending to possess people.     {
      "id": "tzitzimitl-c01",

    {
      "id": "tzitzimitl-c02",
      "source_id": "tzitzimitl-s1",
      "quote": "They were depicted as skeletal female figures wearing skirts often with skull and crossbones designs.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka digambarkan sebagai sosok perempuan berwujud kerangka yang memakai rok, sering bermotif tengkorak dan tulang bersilang.",

      "id": "tzitzimitl-c03",
      "source_id": "tzitzimitl-s1",
      "quote": "In post-conquest descriptions they are often described as \"demons\" or \"devils\", but this does not necessarily reflect their function",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {

    },
    {
      "id": "tzitzimitl-c04",
      "source_id": "tzitzimitl-s1",
      "quote": "The Tzitzimimeh were female deities and related to fertility.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tzitzimimeh adalah dewi-dewi yang berhubungan dengan kesuburan.",
        "en": "The Tzitzimimeh were female deities related to fertility."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tzitzimitl-c05",
      "source_id": "tzitzimitl-s1",
      "quote": "they were worshipped by midwives and parturient women",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka dipuja oleh bidan dan perempuan yang akan melahirkan.",
        "en": "They were worshipped by midwives and women in childbirth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tzitzimitl-c06",
      "source_id": "tzitzimitl-s1",
      "quote": "The leader of the tzitzimimeh was the goddess Itzpapalotl, who was the ruler of Tamoanchan, the paradise where the Tzitzimimeh resided.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pemimpin mereka adalah dewi Itzpapalotl, penguasa Tamoanchan, surga tempat Tzitzimimeh tinggal.",
        "en": "Their leader was the goddess Itzpapalotl, ruler of Tamoanchan, the paradise where the Tzitzimimeh lived."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tzitzimitl-c07",
      "source_id": "tzitzimitl-s1",
      "quote": "This was interpreted as the Tzitzimimeh attacking the Sun, thus causing the belief that during a solar eclipse, the tzitzimime would descend to the earth and possess men.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bintang yang tampak saat gerhana ditafsirkan sebagai Tzitzimimeh yang menyerang Matahari, sehingga dipercaya mereka turun ke bumi dan merasuki manusia.",
        "en": "Stars seen during an eclipse were read as the Tzitzimimeh attacking the Sun, leading to the belief that they descended to earth and possessed people."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tzitzimitl-c08",
      "source_id": "tzitzimitl-s1",
      "quote": "It was said that if the Aztecs could not start a bow fire in the empty chest cavity of a sacrificed human at the end of a 52-year calendar round, the fifth sun would end and they would descend to devour the last of men.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Konon, bila api tidak bisa dinyalakan di rongga dada korban persembahan pada akhir siklus kalender 52 tahun, matahari kelima akan berakhir dan mereka turun untuk memangsa manusia terakhir.",
        "en": "It was said that if fire could not be kindled in a sacrificed person's chest at the end of a 52-year calendar round, the fifth sun would end and they would descend to devour the last people."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tzitzimitl-c09",
      "source_id": "tzitzimitl-s1",
      "quote": "The Tzitzimimeh were also feared during other ominous periods of the Aztec world, such as during the five unlucky days called Nemontemi",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tzitzimimeh juga ditakuti pada masa-masa buruk lain, seperti lima hari sial yang disebut Nemontemi.",
        "en": "The Tzitzimimeh were also feared in other ominous periods, such as the five unlucky days called Nemontemi."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tzitzimitl-c10",
      "source_id": "tzitzimitl-s1",
      "quote": "The Tzitzimimeh had a double role in Aztec religion: they were protectresses of the feminine and progenitresses of mankind.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tzitzimimeh berperan ganda: pelindung kaum perempuan sekaligus leluhur umat manusia.",
        "en": "The Tzitzimimeh had a double role: protectors of women and ancestresses of humankind."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tzitzimitl-c11",
      "source_id": "tzitzimitl-s1",
      "quote": "Tzitzimitl was one of 8 monsters from around the globe to appear as antagonists in the Be Cool, Scooby-Doo! episode titled \"Mysteries On The Disorient Express.\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tzitzimitl termasuk 8 monster yang menjadi lawan dalam episode Be Cool, Scooby-Doo! berjudul Mysteries On The Disorient Express.",
        "en": "Tzitzimitl was one of 8 monsters appearing as antagonists in the Be Cool, Scooby-Doo! episode Mysteries On The Disorient Express."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tzitzimitl-c12",
      "source_id": "tzitzimitl-s2",
      "quote": "しかし、ツィツィミメは一般的に女性と考えられている。『マリアベッキアーノ絵文書』のツィツィミメは骸骨の姿をして貝殻の縁取りのスカートを身につけている。",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tzitzimime umumnya dianggap perempuan; dalam Codex Magliabechiano mereka digambarkan sebagai kerangka yang memakai rok berhias kerang.",
        "en": "The Tzitzimime are generally thought of as female; in the Codex Magliabechiano they appear as skeletons wearing skirts edged with shells."
      }
    }
```

```json
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Apu adalah roh atau dewa gunung yang dipuja dalam agama tradisional Andes di Peru dan Bolivia; dalam bahasa Quechua kata ini berarti \"Tuan\".",
    "en": "An apu is a mountain spirit or deity revered in the traditional religion of the Andes of Peru and Bolivia; in Quechua the word means \"Lord\".",
    "claim_ids": [
      "apu-c01",
      "apu-c05",
      "apu-c06"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Di kalangan penutur Quechua di Peru selatan, apu atau wamani adalah dewa gunung yang hingga kini dipuja luas. Apu dianggap roh alam paling kuat; mereka bisa laki-laki atau perempuan, sebagian melindungi dan sebagian jahat, paling aktif pada bulan Agustus, dan sering dikaitkan dengan kondor serta leluhur.",
      "en": "Among Quechua speakers of southern Peru, the apu or wamani are mountain deities still widely revered. Apu are thought the most powerful nature spirits; they may be male or female, some protective and some malicious, are most active in August, and are often linked with condors and ancestors.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "apu-c01",
        "apu-c02",
        "apu-c03",
        "apu-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Nama apu paling umum di wilayah Cuzco, sedangkan di Huanuco dipakai awkillu, di Ayacucho wamani, dan di Bolivia machula, achachila, atau mallku. Gunung besar seperti Apu Awsanqati di Peru dan Apu Illimani di Bolivia dipuja di wilayah luas sebagai pelindung.",
      "en": "Apu is the usual name around Cuzco, while awkillu is used in Huanuco, wamani in Ayacucho, and machula, achachila or mallku in Bolivia. Great mountains such as Apu Awsanqati in Peru and Apu Illimani in Bolivia are revered across wide areas as protectors.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "apu-c06",
        "apu-c10"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Pemujaan puncak gunung mungkin sudah ada sejak 2000–900 SM. Apu Campana dipuja dengan pengorbanan manusia dan persembahan oleh Moche, Chimu, dan Inka. Dalam mitos Wiraquchapampa dari desa Q'ero, para apu bersatu mengalahkan pasukan Spanyol dan menyelamatkan Inka yang tersisa.",
      "en": "Mountain worship may go back to 2000–900 BCE. Apu Campana was worshipped with human sacrifices and offerings by the Moche, Chimu and Inca. In the Wiraquchapampa myth of Q'ero village, the united apus defeat a Spanish army and save the surviving Inca.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "apu-c07",
        "apu-c08",
        "apu-c09"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apu_(god)",
      "title": "Apu (god)",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (en)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "en",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    },
    {
      "id": "apu-s2",
      "url": "https://de.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apu_(Berggottheit)",
      "title": "Apu (Berggottheit)",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (de)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "de",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "apu-c01",
      "source_id": "apu-s2",
      "quote": "Apu oder Wamani ist bei der Quechua-Bevölkerung im südlichen Peru die Bezeichnung für die Berggottheiten, die bis heute weithin Verehrung genießen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di kalangan penutur Quechua di Peru selatan, Apu atau Wamani adalah sebutan bagi dewa-dewa gunung yang hingga kini dipuja luas.",
        "en": "Among Quechua speakers of southern Peru, Apu or Wamani is the name for the mountain deities, still widely revered today."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apu-c02",
      "source_id": "apu-s1",
      "quote": "Apu are considered the most powerful of nature spirits, can be considered either masculine or feminine, and have varying personalities, with some being protectors and others being malicious.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Apu dianggap roh alam paling kuat, bisa laki-laki atau perempuan, dan wataknya beragam: ada yang melindungi, ada yang jahat.",
        "en": "Apu are thought the most powerful nature spirits, may be male or female, and vary in character, some protective and some malicious."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apu-c03",
      "source_id": "apu-s1",
      "quote": "Apu are the most active in the month of August and apu can have romantic and familial ties with each other.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Apu paling aktif pada bulan Agustus, dan antar-apu bisa memiliki hubungan asmara maupun keluarga.",
        "en": "Apu are most active in August and can have romantic and family ties with one another."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apu-c04",
      "source_id": "apu-s1",
      "quote": "Apu are frequently associated with condors and are often seen as ancestors.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Apu sering dikaitkan dengan burung kondor dan kerap dipandang sebagai leluhur.",
        "en": "Apu are often linked with condors and seen as ancestors."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apu-c05",
      "source_id": "apu-s1",
      "quote": "Apu means \"Lord\" in Quechua as well as sacred mountain or mountain deity.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam bahasa Quechua, apu berarti \"Tuan\", juga gunung suci atau dewa gunung.",
        "en": "In Quechua, apu means \"Lord\" as well as sacred mountain or mountain deity."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apu-c06",
      "source_id": "apu-s1",
      "quote": "Apu is the most common name for mountain spirits in the Cuzco region of Peru, while they are usually called awkillu in Huanuco, wamani in Ayacucho, and machula, achachila, or mallku in different regions in Bolivia.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Apu adalah nama paling umum untuk roh gunung di wilayah Cuzco; di Huanuco disebut awkillu, di Ayacucho wamani, dan di Bolivia machula, achachila, atau mallku.",
        "en": "Apu is the usual name for mountain spirits around Cuzco; they are called awkillu in Huanuco, wamani in Ayacucho, and machula, achachila or mallku in parts of Bolivia."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apu-c07",
      "source_id": "apu-s1",
      "quote": "The veneration of mountain peaks possibly dates back to the Initial Period of the pre-hispanic Andes, which took place from 2000 to 900 BCE.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pemujaan puncak gunung mungkin bermula pada Periode Awal Andes pra-Hispanik, tahun 2000 hingga 900 SM.",
        "en": "The veneration of mountain peaks may date back to the Initial Period of the pre-Hispanic Andes, 2000 to 900 BCE."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apu-c08",
      "source_id": "apu-s1",
      "quote": "Apu Campana was worshipped and appeased via human sacrifices and through the offerings of food and beverages, by the Moche, Chimu, and Incan Empires.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Apu Campana dipuja dan ditenangkan dengan pengorbanan manusia serta persembahan makanan dan minuman oleh Kekaisaran Moche, Chimu, dan Inka.",
        "en": "Apu Campana was worshipped and appeased with human sacrifices and offerings of food and drink by the Moche, Chimu and Inca empires."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apu-c09",
      "source_id": "apu-s2",
      "quote": "Im Mythos von Wiraquchapampa, erzählt im Quechua-Dorf Q'ero, besiegen die vereinten Apus eine spanische Armee und retten so die letzten wenigen überlebenden Inka",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitos Wiraquchapampa dari desa Quechua Q'ero, para apu bersatu mengalahkan pasukan Spanyol dan menyelamatkan segelintir Inka yang tersisa.",
        "en": "In the Wiraquchapampa myth told in the Quechua village of Q'ero, the united apus defeat a Spanish army and save the last surviving Inca."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apu-c10",
      "source_id": "apu-s2",
      "quote": "Einige größere Berge, darunter z. B. der Apu Awsanqati (Ausangate) in Peru oder der Apu Illimani in Bolivien, werden – neben der panandinen Gottheit Pachamama – über größere Gebiete hinweg verehrt, für welche sie eine Schutzgottheit darstellen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Beberapa gunung besar, seperti Apu Awsanqati di Peru dan Apu Illimani di Bolivia, dipuja di wilayah luas sebagai dewa pelindung, di samping dewi Pachamama.",
        "en": "Some large mountains, such as Apu Awsanqati in Peru and Apu Illimani in Bolivia, are revered across wide areas as protective deities, alongside the goddess Pachamama."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",
      "searched": "Dibaca artikel Wikipedia en, de; belum ada sumber di luar Wikipedia yang dikutip untuk entri ini."

    {
      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Gambar Commons belum dicari pada putaran ini; dijadwalkan pada pemeriksaan gambar terpisah."
    }
  ]
}
```

```json
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Lutin adalah makhluk kecil jenaka sejenis hobgoblin dalam cerita rakyat Prancis, yang perannya mirip roh rumah tangga dan kepercayaannya juga menyebar ke Quebec.",
    "en": "The lutin is a small, mischievous hobgoblin of French folklore, similar to a household spirit, whose belief also spread to Quebec.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "lutin-c01",
      "lutin-c02",

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Lutin adalah sejenis hobgoblin jenaka dalam cerita rakyat dan dongeng Prancis: makhluk kecil mirip manusia yang aktif di malam hari, terutama dikenal di Berry, Normandia, dan Picardie. Di Normandia perannya mirip roh rumah tangga di Inggris, Jerman, dan Skandinavia, sedangkan Ardennes dan Wallonia mengenal roh serupa bernama nuton.",
      "en": "The lutin is a mischievous hobgoblin of French folklore and fairy tales: a small nocturnal humanoid known especially in Berry, Normandy and Picardy. In Normandy it plays a role like the household spirits of England, Germany and Scandinavia, while the Ardennes and Wallonia know a similar spirit called the nuton.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "lutin-c01",
        "lutin-c02",
        "lutin-c11",
        "lutin-c12"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Lutin kadang menjelma kuda berpelana yang disebut Le Cheval Bayard, dan suka menjalin rambut orang atau kuda hingga kusut. Dalam dongeng Le Prince Lutin karya Marie Catherine d'Aulnoy (1697), lutin dapat menjadi tak terlihat sesuka hati dan melintasi alam semesta dalam sekejap.",
      "en": "The lutin sometimes becomes a saddled horse called Le Cheval Bayard and likes to tangle the hair of people or horses. In Marie Catherine d'Aulnoy's fairy tale Le Prince Lutin (1697), the lutin can become invisible at will and cross the universe in a moment.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "lutin-c03",
        "lutin-c04",
        "lutin-c05",
        "lutin-c06"
      ]

    {
      "id": "Namanya tercatat sebagai netun sekitar 1176–1181 dalam karya Chrétien de Troyes dan berasal dari Neptunus, nama dewa laut Romawi. Kepercayaan pada lutin juga menyebar ke Quebec, Kanada, tempat lutin dipercaya berwujud hewan peliharaan; kucing yang seluruhnya putih terutama dicurigai sebagai lutin.",
      "en": "The name is attested as netun around 1176–1181 in Chrétien de Troyes and comes from Neptunus, the Roman sea god's name. Belief in lutins also spread to Quebec, Canada, where they were thought to take the form of pets; completely white cats were especially suspected of being lutins.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "lutin-c01",
      "source_id": "lutin-s1",
      "quote": "is a type of hobgoblin (an amusing goblin) in French folklore and fairy tales.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lutin adalah sejenis hobgoblin (goblin jenaka) dalam cerita rakyat dan dongeng Prancis.",
        "en": "A lutin is a kind of hobgoblin (an amusing goblin) in French folklore and fairy tales."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lutin-c02",
      "source_id": "lutin-s1",
      "quote": "plays a similar role in the folklore of Normandy to household spirits in England, Germany and Scandinavia.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Normandia, perannya mirip roh rumah tangga di Inggris, Jerman, dan Skandinavia.",
        "en": "In Norman folklore it plays a role like the household spirits of England, Germany and Scandinavia."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lutin-c03",
      "source_id": "lutin-s1",
      "quote": "It sometimes takes the form of a horse saddled ready to ride, and in this shape is called Le Cheval Bayard.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kadang ia berwujud kuda berpelana siap ditunggangi, dan dalam wujud ini disebut Le Cheval Bayard.",
        "en": "It sometimes appears as a saddled horse ready to ride, and in this form is called Le Cheval Bayard."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lutin-c04",
      "source_id": "lutin-s1",
      "quote": "Lutins sometimes tangle people's or horses' hair into elf-locks.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lutin kadang menjalin rambut orang atau kuda menjadi kusut.",
        "en": "Lutins sometimes tangle the hair of people or horses into elf-locks."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lutin-c05",
      "source_id": "lutin-s1",
      "quote": "A French fairy tale, \"Le Prince Lutin\", written in 1697 by Marie Catherine d'Aulnoy",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dongeng Prancis Le Prince Lutin ditulis Marie Catherine d'Aulnoy pada 1697.",
        "en": "The French fairy tale Le Prince Lutin was written in 1697 by Marie Catherine d'Aulnoy."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lutin-c06",
      "source_id": "lutin-s1",
      "quote": "You are invisible when you like it; you cross in one moment the vast space of the universe; you rise without having wings",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam dongeng itu, lutin dapat menjadi tak terlihat sesuka hati, melintasi alam semesta dalam sekejap, dan naik tanpa sayap.",
        "en": "In that tale the lutin can become invisible at will, cross the universe in a moment and rise without wings."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lutin-c07",
      "source_id": "lutin-s1",
      "quote": "The name is attested as netun c. 1176–1181 by Chrétien de Troyes in Yvain, the Knight of the Lion",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama ini tercatat sebagai netun sekitar 1176–1181 dalam karya Chrétien de Troyes, Yvain, the Knight of the Lion.",
        "en": "The name is attested as netun around 1176–1181 by Chrétien de Troyes in Yvain, the Knight of the Lion."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lutin-c08",
      "source_id": "lutin-s1",
      "quote": "It comes from Latin Neptunus, the name of the Roman god of the seas, which came to also designate a pagan god in Late Latin.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata ini berasal dari bahasa Latin Neptunus, nama dewa laut Romawi, yang dalam bahasa Latin akhir juga berarti dewa pagan.",
        "en": "It comes from Latin Neptunus, the Roman sea god's name, which in Late Latin also came to mean a pagan god."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lutin-c09",
      "source_id": "lutin-s1",
      "quote": "Belief in lutins also spread to North America, particularly the Canadian province of Quebec, as spirits in the form of either pets (such as dogs or rabbits) or other common animals.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kepercayaan pada lutin menyebar ke Amerika Utara, terutama Quebec, Kanada, sebagai roh berwujud hewan peliharaan atau hewan biasa.",
        "en": "Belief in lutins spread to North America, especially Quebec in Canada, as spirits in the form of pets or other common animals."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lutin-c10",
      "source_id": "lutin-s1",
      "quote": "Completely white cats are especially considered likely to be lutins",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kucing yang seluruhnya putih terutama dianggap mungkin lutin.",
        "en": "Completely white cats are especially thought likely to be lutins."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lutin-c11",
      "source_id": "lutin-s2",
      "quote": "Le lutin est une créature humanoïde nocturne de petite taille, issue au sens strict du folklore et des croyances populaires de certaines régions françaises comme le Berry, la Normandie et la Picardie.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lutin adalah makhluk mirip manusia bertubuh kecil yang aktif di malam hari, berasal dari cerita rakyat daerah Prancis seperti Berry, Normandia, dan Picardie.",
        "en": "The lutin is a small nocturnal humanoid from the folklore of French regions such as Berry, Normandy and Picardy."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lutin-c12",
      "source_id": "lutin-s2",
      "quote": "Les Ardennes et la Wallonie connaissent un génie domestique très proche sous le nom de nuton.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Ardennes dan Wallonia dikenal roh rumah yang sangat mirip bernama nuton.",
        "en": "The Ardennes and Wallonia know a very similar household spirit called the nuton."
      }
```

```json
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Amaru adalah ular atau naga mitos peradaban Andes; dalam mitologi Inka ia ular raksasa berkepala dua yang tinggal di bawah tanah dan di dasar danau.",
    "en": "Amaru is a mythical serpent or dragon of the Andean civilizations; in Inca mythology it is a huge two-headed serpent living underground and at the bottom of lakes.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "amaru-mythology-c01",
      "amaru-mythology-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Andes, amaru (katari dalam bahasa Aymara) adalah ular atau naga mitos. Mitologi Inka menggambarkannya sebagai ular raksasa berkepala dua yang tinggal di bawah tanah, di dasar danau dan sungai; kedua kepalanya ditafsirkan sebagai lambang kegelapan dan terang. Amaru melambangkan ketakterbatasan, pengetahuan, dan pembaruan kosmis, dan menghubungkan dunia fana dengan dunia roh.",
      "en": "In Andean mythology the amaru (katari in Aymara) is a mythical serpent or dragon. Inca mythology describes it as a huge two-headed serpent living underground, at the bottom of lakes and rivers; its two heads are read as darkness and light. Amaru stands for infinity, knowledge and cosmic renewal and links the earthly and spiritual worlds.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "amaru-mythology-c01",
        "amaru-mythology-c02",
        "amaru-mythology-c03",
        "amaru-mythology-c10"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Di Gerbang Matahari Tiwanaku, Bolivia, amaru tampil berkepala burung dan puma, sedangkan pada bejana ritual ia berkaki dan bersayap burung sehingga mirip naga. Menurut Brian S. Bauer, ia sering digambarkan sebagai ular bersayap bermata kristal, berkepala llama, bertanduk taruka, dan berekor ikan.",
      "en": "On the Gateway of the Sun at Tiwanaku, Bolivia, amaru appears with the heads of a bird and a puma, while on ritual vessels it has bird feet and wings so it resembles a dragon. According to Brian S. Bauer it was often shown as a winged serpent with crystalline eyes, a llama head, taruka horns and a fish tail.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "amaru-mythology-c04",
        "amaru-mythology-c05",
        "amaru-mythology-c09"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Amaru dikaitkan dengan air yang mengairi ladang orang Aymara, serta dengan dunia bawah, bumi, dan gempa. Meski biasanya baik, sebagian amaru menunjukkan sisi kejam.",
      "en": "Amaru was linked with the waters that irrigated Aymara fields and with the underworld, the earth and earthquakes. Though usually benevolent, some amarus show a violent side.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "amaru-mythology-c06",
        "amaru-mythology-c07",
        "amaru-mythology-c08"
      ]
    }
  ],
```
