  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "pope-lick-monster-c01",
      "source_id": "pope-lick-monster-s1",
      "quote": "is a legendary part-man, part-goat and part-sheep creature reported to live beneath a railroad trestle bridge over Pope Lick Creek, in the Fisherville neighborhood of Louisville, Kentucky, United States.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pope Lick Monster adalah makhluk legendaris setengah manusia, setengah kambing dan domba yang konon hidup di bawah jembatan kereta di atas Pope Lick Creek, Louisville, Kentucky.",
        "en": "The Pope Lick Monster is a legendary part-man, part-goat, part-sheep creature said to live under a railroad trestle over Pope Lick Creek in Louisville, Kentucky."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pope-lick-monster-c02",
      "source_id": "pope-lick-monster-s1",
      "quote": "According to some accounts, the creature uses either hypnosis or voice mimicry to lure trespassers onto the trestle to meet their death before an oncoming train.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut sebagian kisah, ia memakai hipnosis atau peniruan suara untuk memikat orang ke jembatan hingga tewas tertabrak kereta.",
        "en": "Some accounts say it uses hypnosis or voice mimicry to lure trespassers onto the trestle to die before an oncoming train."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pope-lick-monster-c03",
      "source_id": "pope-lick-monster-s1",
      "quote": "Other stories claim the monster jumps down from the trestle onto the roofs of cars passing beneath it.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah lain menyebut ia melompat dari jembatan ke atap mobil yang lewat di bawahnya.",
        "en": "Other stories say it jumps from the trestle onto the roofs of passing cars."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pope-lick-monster-c04",
      "source_id": "pope-lick-monster-s1",
      "quote": "Other legends hold that the monster is a human-goat hybrid, and that it was a circus freak who vowed revenge after being mistreated.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda lain menyebut ia dulunya penampil sirkus yang bersumpah membalas dendam setelah dianiaya.",
        "en": "Other legends say it was a circus performer who vowed revenge after being mistreated."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pope-lick-monster-c05",
      "source_id": "pope-lick-monster-s1",
      "quote": "The legends have turned the area into a site for legend tripping. There have been a number of deaths and accidents at the trestle since its construction",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda ini menjadikan tempat itu tujuan wisata legenda, dan sejak jembatan dibangun sudah terjadi sejumlah kematian dan kecelakaan di sana.",
        "en": "The legends made the area a legend-tripping site, and there have been several deaths and accidents at the trestle since it was built."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pope-lick-monster-c06",
      "source_id": "pope-lick-monster-s1",
      "quote": "in reality, the bridge carries a major rail artery into Louisville.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {

      "source_id": "pope-lick-monster-s2",
      "quote": "souvent surnommé Goat Man, « l'homme-bouc » ou parfois Sheepman, « l'homme-mouton »",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Sihuanaba adalah roh pengubah wujud dalam cerita rakyat Amerika Tengah yang tampil sebagai perempuan cantik berambut panjang, lalu menampakkan wajah tengkorak atau kepala kuda.",
    "en": "The Sihuanaba is a shapeshifting spirit of Central American folklore that appears as a beautiful long-haired woman, then reveals a skull or horse face.",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Sihuanaba, juga disebut Siguanaba, Cigua, atau Cegua, adalah roh pengubah wujud dalam cerita rakyat Amerika Tengah, dengan varian di sebagian Meksiko. Ia tampil sebagai perempuan menarik berambut panjang yang sering terlihat mencuci atau mandi di sungai. Ia memikat pria yang sendirian ke tempat terpencil, lalu menampakkan wajah aslinya, tengkorak tanpa daging atau kepala kuda, yang membuat korban mati ketakutan atau menjadi gila.",
      "en": "The Sihuanaba, also called Siguanaba, Cigua or Cegua, is a shapeshifting spirit of Central American folklore with variants in parts of Mexico. She appears as an attractive long-haired woman, often seen washing or bathing in rivers. She lures lone men to remote places, then reveals her true face, a fleshless skull or a horse's head, which makes victims die of fright or go mad.",

        "sihuanaba-c03",

        "sihuanaba-c07",
        "sihuanaba-c08"

    },
    {
      "id": "Para ahli menduga legenda ini berasal dari Spanyol dan dibawa ke Amerika Latin pada masa kolonial, ketika penjajah memakainya sebagai alat kendali sosial atas penduduk pribumi dan mestizo.",
      "en": "Scholars suggest the legend came from Spain in the colonial period, when colonists used it as a tool of social control over Indigenous and mestizo people.",

      "source_id": "sihuanaba-s1",

    "id": "Soucouyant adalah nenek sihir pengubah wujud dalam cerita rakyat Karibia yang pada malam hari melepas kulitnya, terbang sebagai bola api, dan mengisap darah orang yang tidur.",
    "en": "The soucouyant is a shape-shifting hag of Caribbean folklore who sheds her skin at night, flies as a fireball and sucks the blood of sleepers.",

    {
      "id": "Soucouyant adalah nenek sihir pengubah wujud dan pengisap darah dalam cerita rakyat Karibia; di Guyana, Belize, dan Jamaika ia disebut Ol' Higue. Pada siang hari ia tampak sebagai orang tua penyendiri. Pada malam hari ia melepas kulit keriputnya, menaruhnya di lesung, lalu terbang sebagai bola api untuk mengisap darah orang yang tidur, meninggalkan memar biru kehitaman. Darah itu ditukarnya dengan kekuatan jahat dari Bazil, iblis penghuni pohon kapuk.",
      "en": "The soucouyant is a shape-shifting, blood-sucking hag of Caribbean folklore, called Ol' Higue in Guyana, Belize and Jamaica. By day she looks like a reclusive old person.       "id": "Untuk membongkarnya, orang menaburkan beras di sekitar rumah atau persimpangan desa, karena soucouyant terpaksa memungutinya butir demi butir. Di Trinidad dan Tobago, salib di jendela dipercaya melindungi rumah darinya.",
      "en": "To expose her, people heap rice around the house or the village crossroads, since the soucouyant must gather it grain by grain. In Trinidad and Tobago a cross in the window is believed to protect a house from her.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Squonk adalah makhluk mitos dari hutan Pennsylvania utara yang selalu menangis karena kulitnya buruk, dan dapat larut menjadi air mata bila terpojok.",
    "en": "The squonk is a mythical creature of northern Pennsylvania's forests that weeps constantly over its ugly skin and can dissolve into tears when cornered.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "squonk-c01",
      "squonk-c04",

    {
      "id": "Squonk adalah makhluk mitos yang konon hidup di hutan hemlock Pennsylvania utara. Kulitnya kebesaran dan penuh kutil serta tahi lalat, sehingga ia selalu sedih dan terus menangis; pemburu dapat melacaknya dari jejak air matanya. Bila terpojok atau ketakutan, ia bisa larut menjadi air mata, dan karena itu diberi \"nama ilmiah\" Lacrimacorpus dissolvens.",
      "en": "The squonk is a mythical creature said to live in the hemlock forests of northern Pennsylvania. Its loose skin is covered in warts and moles, so it is always unhappy and weeps constantly; hunters can track it by its tears. Cornered or frightened, it may dissolve into tears, which earned it the \"scientific name\" Lacrimacorpus dissolvens.",

      "id": "squonk-c01",
      "source_id": "squonk-s1",
      "quote": "The squonk is a mythical creature that is reputed to live in the hemlock forests of northern Pennsylvania in the United States.",

      "statement": {

        "en": "The squonk is a mythical creature said to live in the hemlock forests of northern Pennsylvania, United States."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "squonk-c02",
      "source_id": "squonk-s1",
      "quote": "The first written account of the squonk was from the 1910 book Fearsome Creatures of the Lumberwoods.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Catatan tertulis pertama tentang squonk ada dalam buku Fearsome Creatures of the Lumberwoods (1910).",
        "en": "The first written account of the squonk is in the 1910 book Fearsome Creatures of the Lumberwoods."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "squonk-c03",
      "source_id": "squonk-s1",
      "quote": "Its provenance was described in the next written iteration, in the 1939 book Fearsome Critters. This book suggested that the creatures had migrated from deserts to swamps to finally settle in Pennsylvania.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Buku Fearsome Critters (1939) menyebut squonk bermigrasi dari gurun ke rawa lalu menetap di Pennsylvania.",
        "en": "The 1939 book Fearsome Critters said squonks migrated from deserts to swamps before settling in Pennsylvania."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "squonk-c04",
      "source_id": "squonk-s1",
      "quote": "Because of its misfitting skin, which is covered with warts and moles, it is always unhappy",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kulitnya yang kebesaran dan penuh kutil serta tahi lalat membuatnya selalu sedih.",
        "en": "Its ill-fitting skin, covered in warts and moles, makes it always unhappy."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "squonk-c05",
      "source_id": "squonk-s1",
      "quote": "Hunters who are good at tracking are able to follow a squonk by its tear-stained trail, for the animal weeps constantly. When cornered and escape seems impossible, or when surprised and frightened, it may even dissolve itself in tears.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pemburu bisa melacaknya dari jejak air mata karena ia terus menangis; bila terpojok atau ketakutan, ia bahkan bisa larut menjadi air mata.",
        "en": "Hunters can track it by its trail of tears, since it weeps constantly; cornered or frightened, it may even dissolve into tears."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "squonk-c06",
      "source_id": "squonk-s1",
      "quote": "Johnstown, Pennsylvania celebrates the Squonk at the Squonkapalooza in August.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kota Johnstown, Pennsylvania, merayakan squonk dalam festival Squonkapalooza setiap Agustus.",
        "en": "Johnstown, Pennsylvania, celebrates the squonk at the Squonkapalooza in August."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "squonk-c07",
      "source_id": "squonk-s2",
      "quote": "スクォンクにはLacrimacorpus dissolvensという「学名」がつけられている。これはラテン語で「涙」「体」「溶ける」の意味の語を羅列したものである。",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Squonk diberi \"nama ilmiah\" Lacrimacorpus dissolvens, rangkaian kata Latin untuk \"air mata\", \"tubuh\", dan \"larut\".",
        "en": "The squonk is given the \"scientific name\" Lacrimacorpus dissolvens, Latin words for \"tear\", \"body\" and \"dissolve\"."
      }
    }

    "id": "Trauco adalah makhluk kecil mirip kurcaci dalam mitologi Chilote di Chiloé, Chili, yang memikat perempuan dan dijadikan penjelasan atas kehamilan tanpa ayah.",
    "en": "The Trauco is a small dwarf-like being of Chilote mythology in Chiloé, Chile, who seduces women and is blamed for pregnancies without a known father.",

      "trauco-c01",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Chilote di Chiloé, Chili, Trauco adalah makhluk kecil mirip kurcaci yang tinggal jauh di dalam hutan. Wajahnya buruk, tingginya tidak lebih dari 80 cm, dan kakinya berupa tunggul tanpa telapak. Ia anak dewa ular Coi Coi-Vilu, istrinya adalah Fiura, dan ia membawa tongkat bengkok pahueldún serta kapak batu kecil untuk memukul pohon.",
      "en": "In the Chilote mythology of Chiloé, Chile, the Trauco is a small dwarf-like being living deep in the forest. He has an ugly face, stands no more than 80 cm tall and has stumps instead of feet. He is a child of the snake god Coi Coi-Vilu, his wife is the Fiura, and he carries a twisted pahueldún staff and a small stone hatchet for striking trees.",

        "trauco-c04",

      "id": "Trauco punya daya pikat kuat terhadap perempuan, dan sebagian lelaki Chiloé percaya tatapannya dapat mematikan. Bila perempuan lajang hamil tanpa ayah yang jelas, orang menganggap Trauco ayahnya dan tidak menyalahkan perempuan itu.",
      "en": "The Trauco has a strong pull on women, and some men of Chiloé believe his gaze can kill. When an unmarried woman becomes pregnant without a known father, people name Trauco as the father and do not blame her.",

      "source_id": "trauco-s1",
      "quote": "It has a powerful magnetism that attracts young and middle-aged women. According to myth, the Trauco's wife is the wicked and ugly Fiura.",

      "source_id": "trauco-s1",
      "quote": "When a single woman is pregnant and no one steps forward as the father, people assume that Trauco is the father. Because the creature is irresistible, the woman is considered blameless.",

      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

      "source_id": "trauco-s2",

    "id": "Xiuhcōātl adalah ular api dalam agama Aztek, wujud roh dewa api Xiuhtecuhtli dan senjata Huitzilopochtli; namanya berarti \"ular pirus\".",
    "en": "Xiuhcōātl is the fire serpent of Aztec religion, the spirit form of the fire god Xiuhtecuhtli and a weapon of Huitzilopochtli; its name means \"turquoise serpent\".",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam agama Aztek, Xiuhcōātl adalah ular mitologis, wujud roh dewa api Xiuhtecuhtli, yang kadang digambarkan sebagai atlatl atau senjata Huitzilopochtli. Namanya dalam bahasa Nahuatl berarti \"ular pirus\" dan secara simbolis \"ular api\". Ia ditafsirkan sebagai perwujudan musim kemarau dan senjata matahari; dalam kisah kelahiran Huitzilopochtli, sang dewa bertempur dengan perisai, tombak lempar, dan Xiuhcoatl.",
      "en": "In Aztec religion Xiuhcōātl was a mythological serpent, the spirit form of the fire god Xiuhtecuhtli, sometimes shown as an atlatl or weapon of Huitzilopochtli. Its Nahuatl name means \"turquoise serpent\" and symbolically \"fire serpent\". It is read as the embodiment of the dry season and the weapon of the sun; in the birth story of Huitzilopochtli, the god fights with shield, darts and Xiuhcoatl.",

    {
      "id": "Xiuhcoatl sering muncul dalam seni dan kodeks Aztek, biasanya dengan moncong melengkung ke belakang dan tubuh beruas-ruas yang dililit pita kertas bersimpul, lambang persembahan darah. Ia juga menjadi hiasan punggung pada gambar Xiuhtecuhtli dan Huitzilopochtli, dan mahkota kaisar Aztek tampaknya melambangkan ekornya.",
      "en": "Xiuhcoatl was common in Aztec art and codices, usually with a back-turned snout and a segmented body wrapped in knotted paper strips, a sign of blood offering.     {

      "source_id": "xiuhcoatl-s1",

      "source_id": "xiuhcoatl-s1",

    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Inuit di Greenland serta pesisir Labrador dan Teluk Hudson, Adlet atau Erqigdlet adalah ras makhluk yang tubuh bawahnya seperti anjing dan tubuh atasnya seperti manusia. Mereka berlari cepat, lebih tinggi dari manusia, sering bermusuhan dengan manusia, dan dalam beberapa kisah memakan manusia, meski pertemuan biasanya dimenangkan manusia.",
      "en": "In the Inuit mythology of Greenland and the Labrador and Hudson Bay coasts, the Adlet or Erqigdlet are a race whose lower body is like a dog's and upper body like a man's.       "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut mitos, Adlet lahir ketika seorang perempuan Inuit yang cantik di tepi Teluk Hudson menikahi anjing merah raksasa berkekuatan gaib.",
        "en": "In myth the Adlet were born when a beautiful Inuit woman on the shores of Hudson Bay married a giant red dog with supernatural powers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "adlet-c07",
      "source_id": "adlet-s2",
      "quote": "Neuf mois plus tard, elle donna naissance à dix enfants. Les cinq premiers étaient de petits chiots magnifiques ressemblant à leur père. Cependant, les cinq autres étaient des hybrides féroces étant une combinaison des pires traits des deux parents.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia melahirkan sepuluh anak: lima anak anjing yang mirip ayahnya dan lima makhluk campuran ganas yang mewarisi sifat terburuk kedua orang tuanya.",
        "en": "She bore ten children: five puppies like their father and five fierce hybrids with the worst traits of both parents."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "adlet-c08",
      "source_id": "adlet-s3",
      "quote": "Пятеро из десяти детей женщины были волками, которые, согласно мифу, пересекли море и дали начало европейским народам.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut satu versi, lima dari sepuluh anak itu adalah serigala yang menyeberangi laut dan menjadi leluhur bangsa-bangsa Eropa.",
        "en": "In one version, five of the ten children were wolves who crossed the sea and became the ancestors of the European peoples."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [
    {
      "topic": {
        "id": "Nasib lima anak lainnya",
        "en": "Fate of the other five children"
      },
      "positions": [
        {
          "summary": {
            "id": "Anak anjing yang mirip ayahnya.",
            "en": "Puppies like their father."
          },
          "claim_ids": [

          ]
        },
        {

            "en": "Wolves who became ancestors of the European peoples."
          },
          "claim_ids": [

          ]
        }
      ]
    }
  ],

      "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Inuit, akhlut adalah hewan gabungan mirip orca yang berubah menjadi serigala ketika naik ke darat, dan kadang digambarkan sebagai campuran serigala dan orca. Setelah berkeliaran di darat ia bisa kembali ke laut dan menjadi paus lagi. Makhluk ini dikatakan sangat ganas dan memburu manusia serta hewan lain.",
      "en": "In Inuit folklore the akhlut is an orca-like composite animal that turns into a wolf when it comes ashore, sometimes shown as a wolf-orca hybrid. After roaming the land it can return to the sea and become a whale again. It is said to be very fierce and to hunt humans and other animals.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Apotamkin adalah monster laut bertaring berambut merah dalam mitologi Maliseet dan Passamaquoddy yang menyeret anak-anak lengah ke dalam air.",
    "en": "The Apotamkin is a fanged, red-haired sea monster of Maliseet and Passamaquoddy mythology that drags careless children into the water.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "apotamkin-c01",
      "apotamkin-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Maliseet dan Passamaquoddy, Apotamkin adalah monster laut raksasa bertaring yang hidup di Teluk Passamaquoddy, di muara Sungai St. Croix, dan menyeret orang untuk dimakan, terutama anak-anak yang lengah. Ia digambarkan mirip manusia atau bertubuh ikan dengan rambut panjang berwarna merah; dalam beberapa versi ia dulu perempuan yang diubah menjadi ular. Pada 1888 Journal of American Folklore menyamakannya dengan putri duyung.",
      "en": "In Maliseet and Passamaquoddy mythology the Apotamkin is a giant fanged sea monster living in Passamaquoddy Bay at the mouth of the St. Croix River, dragging people in to eat them, especially careless children. It is described as humanoid or fish-bodied with long red hair; in some versions it was a woman turned into a serpent. In 1888 the Journal of American Folklore likened it to a mermaid.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "apotamkin-c01",
        "apotamkin-c02",
        "apotamkin-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Meski disebut monster, ia tidak selalu jahat: keberadaannya mencegah anak masuk ke air tanpa pengawasan, dan orang Passamaquoddy percaya ia mencegah anak terperosok ke es tipis. Legenda Pasifik Barat Laut mengenal Apotamkin berwujud kera tinggi berbulu cokelat jingga, dan versi ini muncul dalam Twilight karya Stephenie Meyer. Apotamkin kadang dianggap versi vampir dari penduduk asli Amerika.",
      "en": "Though called a monster, it is not always evil: its presence keeps children out of the water unsupervised, and the Passamaquoddy believe it stops them from falling through thin ice. Pacific Northwest legends know a tall, ape-like Apotamkin with orange-brown hair, a version that appears in Stephenie Meyer's Twilight. The Apotamkin is sometimes considered a Native American version of a vampire.",

        "apotamkin-c06",
        "apotamkin-c07",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "apotamkin-c01",
      "source_id": "apotamkin-s1",
      "quote": "is a creature in Maliseet and Passamaquoddy mythology. It is described a giant fanged sea monster that lives in the Passamaquoddy Bay, specifically at the mouth of St. Croix River, and pulls people in to eat them, particularly careless children.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Apotamkin adalah makhluk dalam mitologi Maliseet dan Passamaquoddy: monster laut raksasa bertaring di Teluk Passamaquoddy, di muara Sungai St. Croix, yang menyeret orang untuk dimakan, terutama anak-anak yang lengah.",
        "en": "The Apotamkin is a creature of Maliseet and Passamaquoddy mythology: a giant fanged sea monster of Passamaquoddy Bay, at the mouth of the St. Croix River, that drags people in to eat them, especially careless children."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apotamkin-c02",
      "source_id": "apotamkin-s1",
      "quote": "The Apotamkin is sometimes described as being humanoid, and sometimes as having a fully fish body with human hair. It is consistently said to have long red hair, and in some versions, it was a human woman before being changed into a serpent.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kadang ia digambarkan mirip manusia, kadang bertubuh ikan dengan rambut manusia; rambutnya selalu disebut panjang dan merah, dan dalam beberapa versi ia dulu perempuan yang diubah menjadi ular.",
        "en": "It is sometimes humanoid, sometimes fish-bodied with human hair; its hair is always long and red, and in some versions it was a woman turned into a serpent."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apotamkin-c03",
      "source_id": "apotamkin-s1",
      "quote": "In 1888, the Journal of American Folklore described it as similar to a mermaid.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada 1888 Journal of American Folklore menggambarkannya mirip putri duyung.",
        "en": "In 1888 the Journal of American Folklore described it as mermaid-like."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apotamkin-c04",
      "source_id": "apotamkin-s1",
      "quote": "Its presence deters children from entering the water without parental supervision, avoiding injuries or death. The Passamaquoddy tribe believe that the Apotamkin prevents children from falling through thin ice.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Keberadaannya mencegah anak masuk ke air tanpa pengawasan; orang Passamaquoddy percaya Apotamkin mencegah anak terperosok ke es tipis.",
        "en": "Its presence keeps children from entering the water unsupervised; the Passamaquoddy believe the Apotamkin keeps children from falling through thin ice."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apotamkin-c05",
      "source_id": "apotamkin-s1",
      "quote": "Legends from the Pacific Northwest also have a monster called an Apotamkin. This version is tall and apelike as opposed to aquatic, with orange-brown hair and fur.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda Pasifik Barat Laut juga mengenal Apotamkin, tetapi berwujud tinggi seperti kera dengan bulu cokelat jingga.",
        "en": "Pacific Northwest legends also have an Apotamkin, a tall, ape-like version with orange-brown hair."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apotamkin-c06",
      "source_id": "apotamkin-s1",
      "quote": "Both the Northeast and Northwest versions of the Apotamkin were bogyman figures used to instill fear into children to keep them from venturing dangerous areas alone.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kedua versi dipakai sebagai momok untuk menakut-nakuti anak agar tidak pergi sendirian ke tempat berbahaya.",
        "en": "Both versions served as bogeymen to keep children from going into dangerous places alone."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "apotamkin-c07",
      "source_id": "apotamkin-s1",
      "quote": "The Pacific Northwest version of the Apotamkin appears in Stephenie Meyer's Twilight",

      "statement": {

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Cocijo adalah dewa petir dan hujan peradaban Zapotek di Meksiko selatan, dewa terpenting mereka, yang menurut mitos menciptakan dunia dari napasnya.",
    "en": "Cocijo is the lightning and rain god of the Zapotec civilization of southern Mexico, their most important deity, who in myth created the world from his breath.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "cocijo-c01",

    {
      "id": "Cocijo adalah dewa petir peradaban Zapotek pra-Kolumbus di Meksiko selatan; dalam bahasa Zapotek kata cocijo juga berarti \"petir\". Karena kaitannya dengan hujan, ia dewa terpenting orang Zapotek. Dalam mitos mereka, ia menciptakan matahari, bulan, bintang, musim, daratan, sungai, tumbuhan, hewan, serta siang dan malam dari embusan napasnya.",
      "en": "Cocijo is the lightning god of the pre-Columbian Zapotec civilization of southern Mexico; in Zapotec the word cocijo also means \"lightning\". Because of his link with rain he was the most important Zapotec god. In their myth he created the sun, moon, stars, seasons, land, rivers, plants, animals, and day and night from his breath.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "cocijo-c01",
        "cocijo-c03",
        "cocijo-c04",
        "cocijo-c05"

    {
      "id": "Dalam seni Zapotek ia berwajah hewan dengan moncong lebar dan lidah ular bercabang, dan gambarnya muncul sejak Fase Monte Albán II. Ia sepadan dengan dewa hujan Mesoamerika lain: Chaac bagi orang Maya, Tláloc bagi orang Nahua, dan Dzahui bagi orang Mixtek.",
      "en": "In Zapotec art he has an animal face with a wide snout and a forked serpent tongue, and his images appear from the Monte Albán II phase. He corresponds to other Mesoamerican rain gods: Chaac of the Maya, Tláloc of the Nahua and Dzahui of the Mixtecs.",

      "quote": "He has attributes characteristic of similar Mesoamerican deities associated with rain, thunder and lightning, such as Tlaloc of central Mexico, and Chaac (or Chaak) of the Maya civilization.",

    {
      "id": "cocijo-c03",
      "source_id": "cocijo-s1",

    {
      "id": "cocijo-c04",
      "source_id": "cocijo-s1",
      "quote": "Cocijo was the most important deity among the pre-Columbian Zapotecs because of his association with rainfall.",

      "source_id": "cocijo-s1",
      "quote": "In Zapotec myth, he made the sun, moon, stars, seasons, land, mountains, rivers, plants and animals, and day and night by exhaling and creating everything from his breath.",

      "context": "religious-tradition",

        "en": "In Zapotec myth he made the sun, moon, stars, seasons, land, mountains, rivers, plants, animals, and day and night from his breath."

      "id": "cocijo-c06",
      "source_id": "cocijo-s1",
      "quote": "In Zapotec art Cocijo is represented with a zoomorphic face with a wide, blunt snout and a long forked serpentine tongue.",

      "source_id": "cocijo-s2",
      "quote": "Las representaciones de Pitao Cocijo aparecen desde la Fase Monte Albán II, en el período de consolidación de la cultura zapoteca",

      "statement": {

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Duppy adalah sebutan Karibia untuk hantu atau roh, umumnya dianggap roh jahat yang membawa kemalangan dan menghantui orang pada malam hari.",
    "en": "Duppy is the Caribbean word for a ghost or spirit, generally seen as a malevolent spirit that brings misfortune and haunts people at night.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "duppy-c01",
      "duppy-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Duppy adalah kata asal Afrika yang dipakai di pulau-pulau Karibia seperti Bahama, Barbados, dan Jamaika untuk hantu atau roh; di banyak pulau Antilles Kecil roh ini disebut jumbie. Duppy umumnya dianggap roh jahat yang membawa kemalangan dan menghantui orang pada malam hari. Rolling Calf yang berbelit rantai, kuda berkaki tiga, dan Ol' Hige termasuk roh yang paling jahat.",
      "en": "Duppy is a word of African origin used on Caribbean islands such as the Bahamas, Barbados and Jamaica for a ghost or spirit; on many islands of the Lesser Antilles these spirits are called jumbies. Duppy are generally seen as malevolent spirits that bring misfortune and haunt people at night. The chained Rolling Calf, the three-footed horse and Ol' Hige are among the most malicious.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "duppy-c01",
        "duppy-c02",
        "duppy-c03",
        "duppy-c04",
        "duppy-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Asal katanya diperdebatkan: dari bahasa Ga, mengingat banyak tradisi Afrika di Jamaika berasal dari orang Ashanti; dari Dapaa, hari khusus dalam kalender Akan; atau dari kata patois Jamaika yang asal-usulnya tidak pasti.",
      "en": "Its origin is debated: from the Ga language, since much African tradition in Jamaica comes from the Ashanti; from Dapaa, a special day in the Akan calendar; or from a Jamaican patois word of uncertain origin.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "duppy-c05",
        "duppy-c06",
        "duppy-c08"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "duppy-c01",
      "source_id": "duppy-s1",
      "quote": "Duppy is a word of African origin commonly used in various Caribbean Islands, including The Bahamas, Barbados and Jamaica, meaning ghost or spirit.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Duppy adalah kata asal Afrika yang dipakai di berbagai pulau Karibia, termasuk Bahama, Barbados, dan Jamaika, dengan arti hantu atau roh.",
        "en": "Duppy is a word of African origin used across Caribbean islands, including the Bahamas, Barbados and Jamaica, meaning ghost or spirit."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "duppy-c02",
      "source_id": "duppy-s1",
      "quote": "Duppy are generally regarded as malevolent spirits who bring misfortune and woe on those they set upon. They are said to mostly come out and haunt people at night, and people from around the islands claim to have seen them.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Duppy umumnya dianggap roh jahat yang membawa kemalangan; mereka terutama keluar dan menghantui orang pada malam hari.",
        "en": "Duppy are generally seen as malevolent spirits that bring misfortune; they mostly come out to haunt people at night."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "duppy-c03",
      "source_id": "duppy-s1",
      "quote": "The \"Rolling Calf\" (a scary creature said to have chains around its body), \"Three footed horse\", and \"Ol' Hige\" are examples of the more malicious spirits.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rolling Calf (makhluk menakutkan berbelit rantai), Three footed horse (kuda berkaki tiga), dan Ol' Hige adalah contoh roh yang lebih jahat.",
        "en": "The Rolling Calf (a frightening creature bound in chains), the Three footed horse and Ol' Hige are examples of the more malicious spirits."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "duppy-c04",
      "source_id": "duppy-s1",
      "quote": "In many of the islands of the Lesser Antilles, duppy are known as jumbies.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di banyak pulau Antilles Kecil, duppy disebut jumbie.",
        "en": "On many islands of the Lesser Antilles, duppy are called jumbies."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "duppy-c05",
      "source_id": "duppy-s1",
      "quote": "The word duppy originates from the Ga language as most of the African folklore and culture in Jamaica comes from the Ashanti people",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata duppy berasal dari bahasa Ga, karena sebagian besar cerita rakyat Afrika di Jamaika berasal dari orang Ashanti.",
        "en": "The word duppy comes from the Ga language, as most African folklore in Jamaica comes from the Ashanti people."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "duppy-c06",
      "source_id": "duppy-s1",
      "quote": "It could also originate from the special Akan day Dapaa, which occurs 9 days after the first Monday of the Akan month Fwodwo.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {

      }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Greenbrier Ghost adalah hantu Zona Heaster Shue dari West Virginia, yang menurut kesaksian ibunya mengungkap bahwa ia dibunuh suaminya pada 1897.",
    "en": "The Greenbrier Ghost is the ghost of Zona Heaster Shue of West Virginia, who according to her mother's testimony revealed she had been murdered by her husband in 1897.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "greenbrier-ghost-c01",
      "greenbrier-ghost-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Greenbrier Ghost adalah sebutan populer bagi hantu Elva Zona Heaster Shue, perempuan muda dari Greenbrier County, West Virginia. Zona berkenalan dengan Edward Stribbling Trout Shue pada Oktober 1896. Pada 23 Januari 1897 ia ditemukan tewas di rumahnya, dan juri koroner memutuskan ia meninggal karena penyakit jantung.",
      "en": "The Greenbrier Ghost is the popular name for the ghost of Elva Zona Heaster Shue, a young woman of Greenbrier County, West Virginia. Zona met Edward Stribbling Trout Shue in October 1896. On 23 January 1897 she was found dead at home, and a coroner's jury ruled death by heart disease.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "greenbrier-ghost-c01",
        "greenbrier-ghost-c03",
        "greenbrier-ghost-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ibunya, Mary Jane Heaster, mengaku didatangi arwah Zona yang menyatakan Edward membunuhnya dengan mematahkan lehernya. Berbekal cerita itu ia mendatangi jaksa John Alfred Preston, dan pengadilan akhirnya menyatakan Zona dibunuh suaminya.",
      "en": "Her mother, Mary Jane Heaster, said Zona's spirit told her Edward had killed her by breaking her neck. With that story she went to prosecutor John Alfred Preston, and the court eventually found Zona had been murdered by her husband.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "greenbrier-ghost-c02",
        "greenbrier-ghost-c04",
        "greenbrier-ghost-c05"
      ]

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "greenbrier-ghost-c01",
      "source_id": "greenbrier-ghost-s1",
      "quote": "The Greenbrier Ghost is the name popularly given to the ghost of Elva Zona Heaster Shue, a young woman in Greenbrier County, West Virginia, United States, who was murdered in 1897.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Greenbrier Ghost adalah sebutan populer bagi hantu Elva Zona Heaster Shue, perempuan muda di Greenbrier County, West Virginia, Amerika Serikat, yang dibunuh pada 1897.",
        "en": "The Greenbrier Ghost is the popular name for the ghost of Elva Zona Heaster Shue, a young woman of Greenbrier County, West Virginia, United States, murdered in 1897."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "greenbrier-ghost-c02",
      "source_id": "greenbrier-ghost-s1",
      "quote": "Initially judged a death by natural causes, the court later declared that the woman had been murdered by her husband, following testimony by the victim's mother, Mary Jane Heaster, in which she claimed that her daughter's spirit revealed the true cause of death.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kematiannya awalnya dianggap wajar, tetapi pengadilan kemudian menyatakan ia dibunuh suaminya, setelah ibunya, Mary Jane Heaster, bersaksi bahwa arwah putrinya mengungkap penyebab kematian sebenarnya.",
        "en": "First ruled a natural death, the court later found she had been murdered by her husband, after her mother Mary Jane Heaster testified that her daughter's spirit revealed the true cause of death."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "greenbrier-ghost-c03",
      "source_id": "greenbrier-ghost-s1",
      "quote": "On January 23, 1897, Zona Shue was found dead in her home. A coroner's jury rendered a verdict of \"death by heart disease\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada 23 Januari 1897 Zona Shue ditemukan tewas di rumahnya, dan juri koroner memutuskan ia meninggal karena penyakit jantung.",
        "en": "On 23 January 1897 Zona Shue was found dead at home, and a coroner's jury ruled death by heart disease."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "greenbrier-ghost-c04",
      "source_id": "greenbrier-ghost-s1",
      "quote": "According to Mary Jane's story, Zona insisted that Edward had killed her by breaking her neck.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut cerita Mary Jane, arwah Zona bersikeras bahwa Edward membunuhnya dengan mematahkan lehernya.",
        "en": "According to Mary Jane, Zona's spirit insisted Edward had killed her by breaking her neck."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "greenbrier-ghost-c05",
      "source_id": "greenbrier-ghost-s1",
      "quote": "Armed with the story allegedly told to her by the ghost, Mary Jane Heaster visited the local prosecutor, John Alfred Preston",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Berbekal cerita dari hantu itu, Mary Jane Heaster mendatangi jaksa setempat, John Alfred Preston.",
        "en": "Armed with the ghost's story, Mary Jane Heaster went to the local prosecutor, John Alfred Preston."
      }

      "source_id": "greenbrier-ghost-s2",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Hodag adalah makhluk rekaan cerita rakyat Wisconsin yang bertanduk banteng dan berduri di punggung, terkenal sejak \"penemuan\" di Rhinelander pada 1893.",
    "en": "The hodag is an invented creature of Wisconsin folklore with bull horns and back spines, famous since its \"discovery\" in Rhinelander in 1893.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "hodag-c01",
      "hodag-c04",
      "hodag-c07"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Amerika, hodag adalah makhluk menakutkan mirip karnivora besar bertanduk banteng dengan deretan duri melengkung di punggung. Ia konon lahir dari abu sapi jantan yang dibakar, sebagai penjelmaan penderitaan hewan itu; dalam beberapa legenda sapi itu milik Paul Bunyan, penebang kayu raksasa. Menurut cerita, hodag punah setelah makanan utamanya, bulldog putih, menjadi langka.",
      "en": "In American folklore the hodag is a fearsome critter like a large bull-horned carnivore with curved spines down its back. It was said to rise from the ashes of cremated oxen as the embodiment of their suffering; in some legends the ox belonged to Paul Bunyan, the giant lumberjack. According to the tale, the hodag died out when its main food, white bulldogs, grew scarce.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hodag-c01",
        "hodag-c02",
        "hodag-c06",
        "hodag-c08"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Hodag erat dengan kota Rhinelander, Wisconsin. Pada 1893 surat kabar memberitakan penemuan hodag di sana, yang ternyata digagas Eugene Shepard, juru ukur tanah dan tukang iseng setempat. Hodag kini dipandang sebagai hewan rekaan dalam cerita rakyat Wisconsin.",
      "en": "The hodag is closely tied to Rhinelander, Wisconsin. In 1893 newspapers reported a hodag discovered there, a stunt started by local land surveyor and prankster Eugene Shepard. The hodag is now seen as an invented animal of Wisconsin folklore.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hodag-c03",
        "hodag-c04",
        "hodag-c05",
        "hodag-c07"
      ]
    }

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "hodag-c01",
      "source_id": "hodag-s1",
      "quote": "In American folklore, the hodag is a fearsome critter resembling a large bull-horned carnivore with a row of thick curved spines down its back.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Amerika, hodag adalah makhluk menakutkan mirip karnivora besar bertanduk banteng dengan deretan duri melengkung di punggungnya.",
        "en": "In American folklore the hodag is a fearsome critter like a large bull-horned carnivore with a row of thick curved spines down its back."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hodag-c02",
      "source_id": "hodag-s1",
      "quote": "The hodag was said to be born from the ashes of cremated oxen, as the incarnation of the accumulation of abuse the animals had suffered at the hands of their masters.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hodag konon lahir dari abu sapi jantan yang dibakar, sebagai penjelmaan penderitaan hewan-hewan itu di tangan tuannya.",
        "en": "The hodag was said to rise from the ashes of cremated oxen, embodying the abuse they had suffered from their masters."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hodag-c03",
      "source_id": "hodag-s1",
      "quote": "The history of the hodag is strongly tied to Rhinelander, Wisconsin, where it was claimed to have been discovered. The hodag has figured prominently in early Paul Bunyan stories.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sejarah hodag erat dengan Rhinelander, Wisconsin, tempat ia diklaim ditemukan, dan ia menonjol dalam kisah awal Paul Bunyan.",
        "en": "The hodag's history is tied to Rhinelander, Wisconsin, where it was claimed to be found, and it features in early Paul Bunyan stories."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hodag-c04",
      "source_id": "hodag-s1",
      "quote": "In 1893, newspapers reported the discovery of a hodag in Rhinelander, Wisconsin.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada 1893 surat kabar memberitakan penemuan hodag di Rhinelander, Wisconsin.",
        "en": "In 1893 newspapers reported the discovery of a hodag in Rhinelander, Wisconsin."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hodag-c05",
      "source_id": "hodag-s1",
      "quote": "The reports were instigated by well-known Wisconsin land surveyor, timber cruiser and prankster Eugene Shepard, who rounded up a group of local people to capture the animal.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Berita itu digagas Eugene Shepard, juru ukur tanah dan tukang iseng terkenal di Wisconsin, yang mengumpulkan warga untuk menangkap makhluk itu.",
        "en": "The reports were started by Eugene Shepard, a well-known Wisconsin land surveyor and prankster who gathered locals to capture the animal."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hodag-c06",
      "source_id": "hodag-s1",
      "quote": "It became extinct after its main food source, all white bulldogs, became scarce in the area.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut cerita, hodag punah setelah makanan utamanya, anjing bulldog putih, menjadi langka di daerah itu.",
        "en": "According to the tale, it died out after its main food, white bulldogs, became scarce in the area."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hodag-c07",
      "source_id": "hodag-s2",
      "quote": "Der Hodag ist ein ausgedachtes Tier, das Teil der Folklore des amerikanischen Bundesstaates Wisconsin ist.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hodag adalah hewan rekaan yang menjadi bagian cerita rakyat negara bagian Wisconsin.",

    "en": "The Horned Serpent is a mythical serpent of many cultures, especially Native American, linked with water, rain, lightning and rebirth.",

      "id": "Ular Bertanduk muncul dalam mitologi banyak budaya, termasuk penduduk asli Amerika, Eropa, dan Timur Dekat. Di Amerika Utara ia menjadi unsur utama Southeastern Ceremonial Complex pada masa prasejarah.",
      "en": "Many cultures, Native American, European and Near Eastern among them, tell of a Horned Serpent. Details differ, but many stories link it with water, rain, lightning, thunder and rebirth. In North American prehistory it was a major element of the Southeastern Ceremonial Complex.",
      "claim_ids": [

    {
      "id": "horned-serpent-q1086222-c01",
      "source_id": "horned-serpent-q1086222-s1",
      "quote": "The Horned Serpent appears in the mythologies of many cultures including Native American peoples, European, and Near Eastern mythology.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ular Bertanduk muncul dalam mitologi banyak budaya, termasuk penduduk asli Amerika, Eropa, dan Timur Dekat.",
        "en": "The Horned Serpent appears in the mythologies of many cultures, including Native American, European and Near Eastern."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "horned-serpent-q1086222-c02",
      "source_id": "horned-serpent-q1086222-s1",
      "quote": "Details vary among cultures, with many of the stories associating the mystical figure with water, rain, lightning, thunder, and rebirth.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rinciannya berbeda antarbudaya, tetapi banyak kisah mengaitkannya dengan air, hujan, petir, guntur, dan kelahiran kembali.",
        "en": "Details vary, but many stories link it with water, rain, lightning, thunder and rebirth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "horned-serpent-q1086222-c03",
      "source_id": "horned-serpent-q1086222-s1",
      "quote": "Horned Serpents were major components of the Southeastern Ceremonial Complex of North American prehistory.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ular Bertanduk adalah unsur utama Southeastern Ceremonial Complex dalam prasejarah Amerika Utara.",
        "en": "Horned Serpents were major elements of the Southeastern Ceremonial Complex of North American prehistory."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "horned-serpent-q1086222-c04",
      "source_id": "horned-serpent-q1086222-s1",
      "quote": "To the Muscogee people, the Horned Serpent is a type of underwater serpent covered with iridescent, crystalline scales and a single, large crystal in its forehead. Both the scales and crystals are prized for their powers of divination.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bagi orang Muscogee, Ular Bertanduk adalah ular bawah air bersisik kristal berkilau dengan sebuah kristal besar di dahinya; sisik dan kristalnya dihargai karena kekuatan meramal.",
        "en": "For the Muscogee the Horned Serpent is an underwater serpent with iridescent crystal scales and one large crystal on its forehead, both prized for divination."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "horned-serpent-q1086222-c05",
      "source_id": "horned-serpent-q1086222-s1",
      "quote": "Jackson Lewis, a Muscogee Creek informant to John R. Swanton, said, \"This snake lives in the water has horns like the stag. It is not a bad snake. ... It does not harm human beings but seems to have a magnetic power over game.\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jackson Lewis, narasumber Muscogee Creek bagi John R. Swanton, mengatakan ular ini hidup di air, bertanduk seperti rusa, tidak jahat, tidak mencelakai manusia, tetapi seolah punya daya tarik atas hewan buruan.",
        "en": "Jackson Lewis, a Muscogee Creek informant to John R. Swanton, said the snake lives in water, has horns like a stag, is not bad and does not harm people, but seems to draw game to it."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "horned-serpent-q1086222-c06",
      "source_id": "horned-serpent-q1086222-s2",
      "quote": "イロコイ諸族（現・イロコイ連邦）の伝承では、ホーンド・ サーペントの怒りが湖に嵐を起こすものと考えられていた。",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam tradisi Iroquois, kemarahan Ular Bertanduk dipercaya menimbulkan badai di danau.",
        "en": "In Iroquois tradition the Horned Serpent's anger was thought to raise storms on the lakes."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "horned-serpent-q1086222-c07",
      "source_id": "horned-serpent-q1086222-s2",
      "quote": "ミシサグナス族の人々は、オンタリオ湖近くの洞窟にはホーンド・サーペントが棲むために決して近づかなかったという。",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orang Mississauga konon tidak pernah mendekati sebuah gua dekat Danau Ontario karena dihuni Ular Bertanduk.",
        "en": "The Mississauga reportedly never went near a cave by Lake Ontario because a Horned Serpent lived there."
      }
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Iara adalah sosok perempuan air dalam mitologi Brasil yang berakar pada tradisi Tupi dan Guaraní, yang menyanyi di sungai dan memikat lelaki ke dasar air.",
    "en": "Iara is a water woman of Brazilian mythology rooted in Tupi and Guaraní tradition who sings in the rivers and lures men to the depths.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "iara-mythology-c01",
      "iara-mythology-c07",
      "iara-mythology-c08"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Iara adalah tokoh mitologi Brasil yang berakar pada mitologi Tupi dan Guaraní; namanya dari bahasa Nheengatu iiyara, \"nyonya air\". Ia sejenis siren yang tinggal di sungai atau danau dan menyanyikan lagu yang tak tertahankan pada malam hari, terutama di bawah sinar bulan. Suaranya memikat para lelaki, yang lalu mengikutinya ke kerajaannya di dasar air.",
      "en": "Iara is a figure of Brazilian mythology rooted in Tupi and Guaraní mythology; her name comes from Nheengatu iiyara, \"lady of the water\". She is a kind of siren who lives in rivers or lakes and sings irresistible songs at night, especially by moonlight. Her voice enchants men, who follow her to her realm at the bottom of the water.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "iara-mythology-c01",
        "iara-mythology-c04",
        "iara-mythology-c06",
        "iara-mythology-c07",
        "iara-mythology-c08"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Iara mungkin berkembang dari kisah Ipupiara, manusia ikan pemakan daging, lalu bercampur dengan mitos siren dan putri duyung Eropa. Sebagian penulis meyakini Iara versi asli berkulit gelap dan berambut hitam seperti penduduk asli, tetapi puisi Romantik abad ke-19 memberinya rambut pirang atau hijau dan mata biru atau hijau.",
      "en": "Iara may have grown from the lore of Ipupiara, a carnivorous fish-man, later blended with the European siren and mermaid. Some writers believe the original Iara was dark-skinned and black-haired like the indigenous people, but 19th-century Romantic poetry gave her blonde or green hair and blue or green eyes.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "iara-mythology-c02",
        "iara-mythology-c03",
        "iara-mythology-c05"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "iara-mythology-c01",
      "source_id": "iara-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "is a figure from Brazilian mythology based on Tupi and Guaraní mythology.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Iara adalah tokoh mitologi Brasil yang berakar pada mitologi Tupi dan Guaraní.",
        "en": "Iara is a figure of Brazilian mythology based on Tupi and Guaraní mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iara-mythology-c02",
      "source_id": "iara-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "The Iara may have developed from the lore of the carnivorous fish-man Ipupiara ... Conflation with the European myth of the siren, or a beautiful mermaid probably is part of the Iara myth as the seductress of the Amazon River.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Iara mungkin berkembang dari kisah Ipupiara, manusia ikan pemakan daging, lalu bercampur dengan mitos siren atau putri duyung Eropa sebagai penggoda Sungai Amazon.",
        "en": "Iara may have grown from the lore of the carnivorous fish-man Ipupiara, later blended with the European siren or mermaid as the seductress of the Amazon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iara-mythology-c03",
      "source_id": "iara-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Some commentators believe the original version of Iara must have been dark-skinned and black-haired, black-eyed, like the indigenous populations. However the Iara in the 19th century were described as blonde and blue-eyed or green-eyed, or even green haired.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian penulis meyakini Iara versi asli berkulit gelap, berambut dan bermata hitam seperti penduduk asli, tetapi pada abad ke-19 ia digambarkan pirang dengan mata biru atau hijau, bahkan berambut hijau.",
        "en": "Some believe the original Iara was dark-skinned with black hair and eyes like the indigenous people, but in the 19th century she was described as blonde with blue or green eyes, or even green hair."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iara-mythology-c04",
      "source_id": "iara-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "The word derives from Nheengatu iiyara, from ií \"water\" + yara \"lord; lady\".",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata ini berasal dari bahasa Nheengatu iiyara, dari ií \"air\" dan yara \"tuan; nyonya\".",
        "en": "The word comes from Nheengatu iiyara, from ií \"water\" + yara \"lord; lady\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iara-mythology-c05",
      "source_id": "iara-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "Later, the poetry by Romantic writers in the 19th century would give her blonde hair, or green hair of the color of vegetation.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Puisi para penulis Romantik abad ke-19 kemudian memberinya rambut pirang atau hijau seperti tetumbuhan.",
        "en": "Nineteenth-century Romantic poetry later gave her blonde hair, or green hair the colour of vegetation."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iara-mythology-c06",
      "source_id": "iara-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "ist in der brasilianischen Mythologie eine Sirene.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Brasil, Iara adalah sejenis siren.",
        "en": "In Brazilian mythology Iara is a siren."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iara-mythology-c07",
      "source_id": "iara-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "Sie hat grüne Augen und lebt in den Flüssen oder Seen, wo sie nachts, vor allem bei Mondschein, unwiderstehliche Lieder singt.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia bermata hijau dan tinggal di sungai atau danau, tempat ia menyanyikan lagu yang tak tertahankan pada malam hari, terutama saat bulan bersinar.",
        "en": "She has green eyes and lives in rivers or lakes, where at night, especially by moonlight, she sings irresistible songs."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "iara-mythology-c08",
      "source_id": "iara-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "Man sagt, sie habe eine so schöne und rührende Stimme, dass sie die Männer damit verzaubert, die ihr in ihr Reich am Grunde des Wassers folgen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Suaranya begitu indah dan menyentuh sehingga memikat para lelaki, yang lalu mengikutinya ke kerajaannya di dasar air.",
        "en": "Her voice is so beautiful and moving that it enchants men, who follow her to her realm at the bottom of the water."
      }
    }
  ],

    "id": "Hantu Lincoln adalah arwah Presiden Abraham Lincoln yang konon menghantui Gedung Putih sejak ia dibunuh pada 1865.",
    "en": "Lincoln's ghost is the spirit of President Abraham Lincoln, said to have haunted the White House since his assassination in 1865.",

    {
      "id": "Hantu Presiden Abraham Lincoln, juga disebut Hantu Gedung Putih, konon menghantui Gedung Putih sejak Lincoln dibunuh pada 1865, dan juga bekas kediamannya di Springfield, Illinois. Di antara yang disebut pernah melihatnya adalah Grace Coolidge, Ratu Wilhelmina, Winston Churchill, dan Theodore Roosevelt.",
      "en": "Abraham Lincoln's ghost, nicknamed the White House Ghost, has reportedly roamed the presidential residence ever since he was assassinated in 1865, and his former homes in Springfield, Illinois, as well. Those said to have seen it include Grace Coolidge, Queen Wilhelmina, Winston Churchill and Theodore Roosevelt.",

        "lincoln-s-ghost-c01",

    {
      "id": "Grace Coolidge disebut orang pertama yang melihatnya, pada 1927, berdiri di jendela Yellow Oval Room. Pada 1942 Ratu Wilhelmina konon melihat Lincoln di depan pintu kamarnya dan pingsan, meski artikel koran Belanda tahun 1996 menyebut ia tidak pingsan. Pada awal 1870-an janda Lincoln, Mary, berfoto di studio William H. Mumler dengan sosok mirip mendiang suaminya di latar belakang, yang oleh Stephen Wagner dijelaskan sebagai pencahayaan ganda.",
      "en": "Grace Coolidge is said to be the first to see it, in 1927, standing at a window of the Yellow Oval Room.   "claims": [
    {
      "id": "lincoln-s-ghost-c01",
      "source_id": "lincoln-s-ghost-s1",
      "quote": "The ghost of U.S. president Abraham Lincoln, also known as the White House Ghost, is said to have haunted the White House since Lincoln's assassination in 1865.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hantu Presiden AS Abraham Lincoln, yang juga disebut Hantu Gedung Putih, konon menghantui Gedung Putih sejak Lincoln dibunuh pada 1865.",
        "en": "The ghost of U.S. president Abraham Lincoln, also called the White House Ghost, is said to have haunted the White House since his assassination in 1865."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lincoln-s-ghost-c02",
      "source_id": "lincoln-s-ghost-s1",
      "quote": "Lincoln's ghost has also been said to haunt many of his former residences in Springfield, Illinois, including his former law office.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hantunya juga dikatakan menghantui bekas kediamannya di Springfield, Illinois, termasuk bekas kantor hukumnya.",
        "en": "His ghost is also said to haunt his former homes in Springfield, Illinois, including his old law office."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lincoln-s-ghost-c03",
      "source_id": "lincoln-s-ghost-s1",
      "quote": "First Lady Grace Coolidge, Queen Wilhelmina of the Netherlands, Prime Minister Winston Churchill, and President Theodore Roosevelt are among those claimed to have stated they saw Lincoln's ghost in the White House.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ibu Negara Grace Coolidge, Ratu Wilhelmina dari Belanda, Perdana Menteri Winston Churchill, dan Presiden Theodore Roosevelt disebut pernah mengaku melihat hantu Lincoln di Gedung Putih.",
        "en": "First Lady Grace Coolidge, Queen Wilhelmina of the Netherlands, Prime Minister Winston Churchill and President Theodore Roosevelt are said to have claimed seeing Lincoln's ghost in the White House."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lincoln-s-ghost-c04",
      "source_id": "lincoln-s-ghost-s1",
      "quote": "The first person reported to have actually seen Lincoln's spirit was First Lady Grace Coolidge, who said she saw the ghost of Lincoln standing at a window in the Yellow Oval Room staring out at the Potomac in 1927.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Orang pertama yang dilaporkan melihatnya adalah Grace Coolidge, yang pada 1927 melihat Lincoln berdiri di jendela Yellow Oval Room menatap Sungai Potomac.",
        "en": "The first person reported to see it was Grace Coolidge, who in 1927 saw Lincoln standing at a window of the Yellow Oval Room gazing at the Potomac."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lincoln-s-ghost-c05",
      "source_id": "lincoln-s-ghost-s1",
      "quote": "Perhaps the most famous incident was in 1942 when Queen Wilhelmina of the Netherlands allegedly heard footsteps outside her White House bedroom and answered a knock on the door, only to see Lincoln in frock coat and top hat standing in front of her (she promptly fainted).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kejadian paling terkenal terjadi pada 1942: Ratu Wilhelmina mendengar langkah kaki, membuka pintu yang diketuk, dan melihat Lincoln bermantel panjang dan bertopi tinggi, lalu pingsan.",
        "en": "The most famous incident came in 1942: Queen Wilhelmina heard footsteps, answered a knock and saw Lincoln in frock coat and top hat, then fainted."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lincoln-s-ghost-c06",
      "source_id": "lincoln-s-ghost-s1",
      "quote": "According to a Dutch 1996 newspaper article she did not faint, but saw the man, who looked slightly familiar to her, disappear before she could say anything.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel koran Belanda tahun 1996, ia tidak pingsan, melainkan melihat lelaki yang agak dikenalnya itu menghilang sebelum ia sempat berbicara.",
        "en": "According to a 1996 Dutch newspaper article, she did not faint but saw the faintly familiar man vanish before she could speak."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lincoln-s-ghost-c07",
      "source_id": "lincoln-s-ghost-s2",
      "quote": "メアリー未亡人は1870年代前半に、心霊写真家であるウィリアム・H・マムラーの撮影スタジオを訪れて写真を撮ってもらったが、その背後には彼女が亡き夫に似ていると認める男の姿が映っていた。",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada awal 1870-an, sang janda Mary berfoto di studio fotografer arwah William H. Mumler; di belakangnya tampak sosok lelaki yang ia akui mirip mendiang suaminya.",
        "en": "In the early 1870s the widowed Mary was photographed at spirit photographer William H. Mumler's studio; behind her appeared a man she said resembled her late husband."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lincoln-s-ghost-c08",
      "source_id": "lincoln-s-ghost-s2",
      "quote": "超常現象専門家のスティーブン・ワグナーはこの写真は二重露光によるものだとしているが、広く配布されている。",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pakar paranormal Stephen Wagner menyebut foto itu hasil pencahayaan ganda, tetapi foto itu tersebar luas.",
        "en": "Paranormal expert Stephen Wagner attributes the photo to double exposure, though it has circulated widely."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Pishtaco adalah sosok momok dalam cerita rakyat Andes, terutama Peru dan Bolivia, yang digambarkan sebagai pembunuh yang mengambil lemak korbannya.",
    "en": "The pishtaco is a bogeyman of Andean folklore, especially in Peru and Bolivia, pictured as a killer who extracts his victims' fat.",
    "claim_ids": [

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Pishtaco adalah sosok momok dalam tradisi Quechua dan Aymara di Andes, terutama Peru dan Bolivia: pembunuh mengerikan, biasanya digambarkan sebagai lelaki kulit putih atau mestizo, yang mengambil lemak korbannya. Ia bersenjata pisau panjang untuk memenggal atau memotong korban, tetapi bisa juga tidak meninggalkan luka sama sekali sementara korban meninggal beberapa hari kemudian.",
      "en": "The pishtaco is a bogeyman of Quechua and Aymara tradition in the Andes, especially Peru and Bolivia: a monstrous killer, usually pictured as a white or mestizo man, who extracts his victims' fat. He carries a long knife for beheading or dismembering, but may also leave no wound at all while the victim dies days later.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "pishtaco-c01",
        "pishtaco-c05",
        "pishtaco-c06",
        "pishtaco-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Namanya di Peru tengah, pishtaco, berarti \"penjagal\" dalam bahasa Quechua; di Altiplano Bolivia ia disebut kharisiri atau lik'ichiri, \"pembuat lemak\". Sosok ini diyakini berasal dari praktik penakluk Spanyol yang memakai lemak jenazah penduduk asli Peru sebagai obat.",
      "en": "In central Peru his name, pishtaco, means \"slaughterer\" in Quechua; in the Bolivian Altiplano he is called kharisiri or lik'ichiri, \"fat-maker\". The figure is thought to stem from Spanish conquistadors using the corpse fat of Indigenous Peruvians as medicine.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "pishtaco-c02",
        "pishtaco-c03",
        "pishtaco-c04"
      ]
    }

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "pishtaco-c01",
      "source_id": "pishtaco-s1",
      "quote": "is a folkloric boogeyman figure in the Andes region of South America, particularly in Peru and Bolivia, which extracts the fat of its victims.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pishtaco adalah sosok momok cerita rakyat di Andes, terutama Peru dan Bolivia, yang mengambil lemak korbannya.",
        "en": "The pishtaco is a folk bogeyman of the Andes, especially Peru and Bolivia, that extracts its victims' fat."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pishtaco-c02",
      "source_id": "pishtaco-s1",
      "quote": "It is believed to have originated in Spanish conquistadors' practice of using Indigenous Peruvians' corpse fat as treatment for wounds and illnesses.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sosok ini diyakini berasal dari praktik penakluk Spanyol yang memakai lemak jenazah penduduk asli Peru sebagai obat luka dan penyakit.",
        "en": "It is thought to stem from Spanish conquistadors using the corpse fat of Indigenous Peruvians to treat wounds and illnesses."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pishtaco-c03",
      "source_id": "pishtaco-s1",
      "quote": "In central Peru it is called pishtaco (in Central and Northern Quechua, meaning \"slaughterer\", from pishtay, \"behead, cut the throat\")",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Peru tengah ia disebut pishtaco, dari bahasa Quechua yang berarti \"penjagal\", dari pishtay, \"memenggal, menggorok leher\".",
        "en": "In central Peru it is called pishtaco, Quechua for \"slaughterer\", from pishtay, \"behead, cut the throat\"."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pishtaco-c04",
      "source_id": "pishtaco-s1",
      "quote": "It is called by the Aymara names kharisiri (var. karisiri \"slaughterer\") or lik'ichiri (\"fat-maker\") in the Bolivian Altiplano",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Altiplano Bolivia ia disebut dengan nama Aymara kharisiri (\"penjagal\") atau lik'ichiri (\"pembuat lemak\").",
        "en": "In the Bolivian Altiplano it has the Aymara names kharisiri (\"slaughterer\") or lik'ichiri (\"fat-maker\")."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pishtaco-c05",
      "source_id": "pishtaco-s1",
      "quote": "He is often given a white male racial profile (or mestizo), often said to be armed with a long knife, with which he beheads, disembowels, or dismembers his victims",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia sering digambarkan sebagai lelaki kulit putih atau mestizo bersenjata pisau panjang untuk memenggal atau memotong-motong korbannya.",
        "en": "He is often pictured as a white or mestizo man armed with a long knife for beheading or dismembering victims."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pishtaco-c06",
      "source_id": "pishtaco-s1",
      "quote": "But the pishtaco may also leave no visible wound on the victim, but cause a fatal condition incurring death after a few days.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pishtaco juga bisa tidak meninggalkan luka sama sekali, tetapi korban meninggal beberapa hari kemudian.",
        "en": "The pishtaco may also leave no visible wound yet cause death after a few days."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "pishtaco-c07",
      "source_id": "pishtaco-s2",
      "quote": "ist in der andinen Tradition der Quechua und Aymara in Peru und Bolivien ein monströser, meist weißer Mörder, der den ermordeten Indigenen das Fett aussaugt.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam tradisi Andes orang Quechua dan Aymara di Peru dan Bolivia, ia pembunuh mengerikan, biasanya kulit putih, yang mengisap lemak penduduk asli yang dibunuhnya.",
        "en": "In the Andean tradition of the Quechua and Aymara in Peru and Bolivia, it is a monstrous, usually white murderer who sucks the fat from the Indigenous people he kills."
      }
    }
  ],
